# Validation register (consolidated)

Generated from the apoc-exe-validation workflow: 11 validators + adversarial refuters (engine lens, EXE lens).
Review status: survived = all refuters failed to refute; refuted = all refuters refuted (see corrected claim); contested = split; not_refuted_by_policy = exe_unbound or low impact (no refuter run).
FINAL VERDICT rule: if refuted/contested and a refuter supplied verdict_should_be != unchanged, the corrected claim must be read before relying on the original.

### v_gov_income / G1-funding-schedule — Weekly Senate funding: tier delta on OLD income F from this week's score W, old F credited first, next income = max(0, min(F+delta, trunc(G/2))) STORED, Government debited the NEW income, 2e9 pre-credit guard
- impact **critical**, validator verdict **match** (confidence high), review **survived**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: LIVE game/state/gamestate.cpp:1781-1829: credit old F unless balance>=2e9 (:1787-1790), delta=oldIncome/modifier (:1808-1809; calculateFundingModifier :2000-2023, data/common_patch/gamestate.xml:16-57, most extreme tier wins), G/2<=0 clears delta and cap=-F else cap=G/2-(F+delta) (:1811-1822), store max(0,F+delta+cap) as player->income (:1823), government->balance -= nextIncome (:1824). HEAD fda36d6c gamestate.cpp:1745-1763 caps only the payment (:1749-1753), stores UNCAPPED income (:1762), transfers the capped payment symmetrically (:1756-1757), no 2e9 guard (D2-D4); HEAD weeklyfundingscreen.cpp:33-37,61,80-85,121 re-derives state in the UI (D7).
- EXE: Org loop credits each org its income (X-COM: F) first, skipped when balance>=2,000,000,000; Government also gets 10% civilian tax. Then tier delta from F (strict inequalities: >400 F/20, >800 F/16, >1600 F/12, >3200 F/8, >6400 F/5, >12800 F/4; <0 -F/15, <-400 -F/10, <-800 -F/5, <-1600 -F/4; sequential ifs so the most extreme match wins); G/2<=0 -> delta 0, cap -F; else cap=min(0,G/2-(F+delta)); income stored = F+delta+cap; Government balance -= new income. Same code in mode 0 (Monday) and mode 1 (new game).

Credit guard CMP EAX,0x77359400 at file 0xF6AA8 and store 0xF6AB2; tiers 0xF6EC7-0xF6FA4; G/2 at 0xF6FD5-0xF6FE2; cap 0xF6FEF-0xF7034; income store 0xF7083; Government debit 0xF70B7. ### v_gov_income / G4-newgame-funding-pass — Funding/economy pass at new game (D18) and the X-COM starting balance/income
- impact **critical**, validator verdict **engine_bug** (confidence high), review **survived**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: startGame() calls updateEndOfWeek(true) (LIVE gamestate.cpp:714, HEAD :713) -> updateOrgFinances() then weeklyPlayerUpdate() unconditionally (LIVE :1738, HEAD :1705), BEFORE initState() runs City::initCity (city.cpp:157 LIVE / :150 HEAD) which is the only place Organisation::buildings is filled. The day-0 updateOrgFinances sees no buildings: no Government income or tax, G=20,000, averageWage forced to 1 (:1896-1897). LIVE then credits old F=80,000, caps and STORES 10,000 (:1789,:1818-1824), Government 20,000->10,000; no agents/bases exist yet so overheads 0 -> X-COM 180,000 / 10,000. HEAD credits the capped payment 10,000 and leaves income 80,000 -> 110,000 / 80,000. initState then runs updateOrgFinances twice (:315-316 LIVE, :314-315 HEAD), lifting the Government to about 2.1M, too late. OBSERVED: 131 logged LIVE-arm campaigns start with funds 180,000, 32 HEAD-arm with 110,000, none with 100,000; tick-0 LIVE saves show X-COM income 10,000, previousWeekIncome 80,000, Government 2.06-2.11M.
- EXE: FUN_000b94c0 runs FUN_000941dc(AL=1) + FUN_000962cc twice (mode 1 skips the funding report, the salary/upkeep screen and the balance commit; payments, tax, market passes, cutoff test, cap and Government debit still run), THEN stores X-COM balance = 140000-10000*d and income = 92000-3000*d. Superhuman (d=4) therefore starts at exactly 100,000 / 80,000; the X-COM side of the passes is discarded; Government-side effects (two F_next debits, park refills, market passes, wage settling) persist (INFERRED persistence).
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE ISO non-4 (CRC 0x4749ffc1): FUN_000b94c0 file 0x11BB64 (Ghidra VA 0xB94C0 = file - 0x626A4; entry bytes 53 51 52 56 57 e8 66 bd ff ff match Ghidra's ENTRY_BYTES). Loop: xor edx,edx at 0x11BBD7; mov eax,ebx(=1) 0x11BBDE; call 0xF6880 at 0x11BBE0; call 0xF8970 (FUN_000962cc) at 0x11BBE6; cmp dx,2; jl at 0x11BBEB. Mode gating inside FUN_000941dc: arg saved at 0xF6889, tests at 0xF7038, 0xF70BD, 0xF70E0. Stores: balance at 0x11BC47, income at 0x11BC5D (constants 140000/92000 at data VA 0xDFDCC/0xDFDD0 = file 0x141470/0x141474, file = VA + 0x616A4). Ghidra decompile confirms do{FUN_000941dc();FUN_000962cc();}while(extraout_DX<2). Callers of FUN_000b94c0: FUN_000b9178 main-menu switch case 1 (0x11BAD8) and case 3 (0x11BB37).
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: All affordability and budget rules: real Superhuman X-COM income is 80,000/week (net about +57,100/week at the start against 22,900 of overheads), the LIVE benchmark plays at about 10,000 (net about -12,900/week); conclusions about what the campaign can afford drawn from LIVE logs are invalid.
- FIX SKETCH: Primary: in startGame() do not call updateOrgFinances()/weeklyPlayerUpdate() (keep updateUfoGrowth()/updateItemMarket() if wanted); let initState()'s two updateOrgFinances() calls be the warm-up (optionally followed by two Government debits of F_next); leave X-COM at the difficulty-patch 100,000/80,000. Stopgap: capture player->balance/income before updateEndOfWeek(true) and restore after (fixes X-COM, removes HEAD's +10,000, leaves the malformed third economy pass: wage-floor cut, expectedWage 1, wage steps x1.05-1.2 over humanCity->buildings with populationWorking 0 -- INFERRED from code, not measured). Land this BEFORE bankruptcy (G8).

### v_relations / F02-scenery-hit-intent-gating — Relation loss from projectile hits on a building (X-COM firer): per-hit vs per-destroyed-tile and intentional vs stray fire
- impact **critical**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence high), review **survived**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: LIVE game/state/city/scenery.cpp:1197-1226 handleCollision: for EVERY hit with !type->commonProperty && building && firerVehicle, updateRelationWithAttacker(false) at :1215 gives owner->firer -5 (def. :1168-1181); the `intentional` flag (:1216-1219: manualFire or AttackBuilding mission) only gates building->underAttack. A destroying hit then adds -20 (updateRelationWithAttacker(true), :1268 in applyDamage, x4 multiplier). HEAD: scenery.cpp updateRelationWithAttacker :1158, handleCollision :1187, call :1205, kill :1258. Totals: any hit -5, any destroyed tile -25, regardless of intent.
- EXE: FUN_00054a28 scenery branch (human city only, building found by collision XY, firer vehicle alive, owner org != firer org): INTENTIONAL fire (hit building == firer's target building at vehicle+0x15A AND byte vehicle+0xCF==2, OR X-COM firer with UI mode [0xD4E28]==9 and firer == selected vehicle): tile survives -> prim(owner,firer,-5); tile destroyed -> prim(owner,firer,-25) in ONE call. STRAY fire: tile survives -> 0; tile destroyed -> prim(owner,firer,-5). Category-6 score is charged separately for X-COM/Alien firers regardless.
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE ISO non-4, FUN_00054a28 physical 0xB70CC (VA 0x54A28+0x626A4). Intentional test: CMP DI,SI at VA 0x5615F (file 0xB8803), CMP byte[EAX+0xCF],2 VA 0x56164 (0xB8808), CMP word[0xD4E28],9 VA 0x5617B (0xB881F). Tile-destroyed branch VA 0x5639A/0x563A3 (0xB8A3E/0xB8A47). -25 call VA 0x563C6 (file 0xB8A6A, imm BBE7FFFFFF @ 0xB8A5B); -5 call VA 0x56431 (file 0xB8AD5, imm BBFBFFFFFF @ 0xB8ACA); stray path VA 0x563FC; alien-city skip VA 0x5605C (0xB8700). destroyed flag stored at VA 0x55FEF from FUN_0005BD08 result.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Dogfights over the city and any fire near buildings: in the EXE stray hits that merely strike a building cost 0 relation (-5 only per destroyed tile) and only deliberate fire (attack-building order, or manual fire by the selected craft) costs -5/-25. From a Superhuman start of about 65 the funding cutoff (<-50) needs 5 intentional destroyed tiles or 24 intentional surviving hits, or 24 stray destroyed tiles; the engine reaches it with 24 stray surviving hits or 5 stray destroyed tiles.
- FIX SKETCH: In Scenery::handleCollision compute `intentional` first (already available). Replace updateRelationWithAttacker calls with: if owner==firer org -> none; else if intentional: delta = destroyed ? -25 : -5 (single adjustRelationTo call with ripple); else: delta = destroyed ? -5 : 0. Remove the separate x4 call in applyDamage (or make it the 'destroyed' signal into this logic). Keep `ScrambleOnUnintentionalHit` only for underAttack. Human city only. Define intentional = manualFire (X-COM, selected craft) || firer mission front == AttackBuilding targeting this building.

HEAD differs from LIVE in scenery.cpp only by activate() additions, so the HEAD line numbers (1158/1187/1205/1258) are right. Holder/target direction is the same in both: owner's view of the firer. What I checked:

- The primitive's argument roles are as the validator says. EAX = holder A, EDX = target B, BX = delta, and the cell is m[A][B] at 0x16EC28 + A*28 + B. At both call sites in FUN_00054a28, A = building owner and B = firer org. So the cell is the owner's view of the firer. The X-COM mirror (m[0][j] = m[j][0]) makes this the Government->X-COM value that funding reads.
- The stray/intentional flow is exactly as claimed.

1. FUN_0006fd9c (UFO2P.EXE, ISO non-4, CRC 0x4749ffc1, confirmed by zlib.crc32 of canonical/UFO2P.EXE; file 0xD2440, prologue 53 51 52 56 57 55 confirmed). It does `mov edx,[eax+0x13f4c8]` (infil[C]), `mov eax,0x64`, `sub edx,0x64`, `call rand16`, `and eax,0xffff`, `cmp eax,edx`, `jge skip`. So it takes over iff rand16(100) < infil-100. rand16 (FUN_0005d1d8, file 0xBF87C) pushes and pops EDX, so infil-100 survives the call. It returns a value mod (n+1), inclusive 0..100. The compare is signed, so for infil <= 100 the roll can never succeed. P = (v-100)/101 above that, with no difficulty term in the function. The same byte sequence exists in UFO2P4.EXE at file 0xD2303.
2. Engine: LIVE and HEAD organisation.cpp are byte-identical. Line 86 reads `randBoundsExclusive(rng,0,200) >= infiltrationValue`. Per library/xorshift.h:133-143, Exclusive(min,max) is Inclusive(min,max-1), so the roll is 0..199 and P = v/200 for any v > 0. `randBoundsInclusive` exists (organisation.cpp:47), so the proposed fix compiles and includes 100.
3. No other writer of the taken-over flag is reachabl

### v_victory_chain / VC-10 — Control Chamber destruction arms a one-shot mass assault of alien-dimension UFOs on the human city
- impact **critical**, validator verdict **engine_missing** (confidence medium), review **survived**, blocked_on_re=True
- ENGINE: Absent. No Control Chamber handling anywhere in game/ or tools/ (grep returns only the UI welcome string). Control Chamber is an ordinary building.
- EXE: FUN_000b32ac sets [0xF3C78]=1 when the destroyed building's function index is 7. At the next invasion-timer expiry in the human city (FUN_0006d384 calls FUN_0006cfb4 with EAX=0) FUN_0006cfb4 calls FUN_00070938, which allocates up to 20 UFOs of types 1-9 round robin from the dimension stock (word table at 0x143D40) into table 0x1A44A4; if fewer than 10 are allocated F3C78 is cleared and nothing happens, else the normal incursion is skipped and an incursion is flagged running. FUN_0006d384's alternate branch then spawns one UFO per step (lowest type first), and clears F3C78 when the allocation is exhausted. In mass mode FUN_0006da88 does not apply the damaged-UFO withdraw percent and uses fixed order bytes (+0x16E=0x32, +0x170=2, +0x171=0x14). No write to the dimension stock was seen in FUN_00070938 or FUN_0006d384.
- EVIDENCE: FUN_000b32ac: CMP [0x244048],7 at file 0x115A4A, MOV [0xF3C78],1 at file 0x115A53. FUN_0006cfb4 (file 0xCF658): F3C78 tests at VA 0x6D074-0x6D0B0 (file 0xCF718-0xCF754); FUN_00070938 file 0xD2FDC (round robin, caps 0x14 and 0xA at its end); FUN_0006d384 file 0xCFA28 (EAX=0 call at VA 0x6D3C6-0x6D3C8, alternate branch gated by F3C78). xr of 0xF3C78: only non-zero writer is VA 0xB33AF; initial image zero (file 0x15531C). FUN_0006da88 file 0xD012C. Contradicts the index/docs 'closed negative' for a Control Chamber city-wide attack.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Endgame risk: once the Control Chamber (building 6) falls, the next incursion (24-72 h later) can be up to 20 UFOs from the dimension stock hitting the human city in one go. Plan base and city defence, and reduce the dimension fleet, before raid 6. Without this the strategy underestimates mid-endgame pressure.
- FIX SKETCH: Add a one-shot flag set when a RaidAliens win has building->function == BUILDINGFUNCTION_CONTROL_CHAMBER. At the next incursion roll, if armed and the alien-city stock of types except Probe yields at least 10 (round robin, max 20), launch them as one incursion and clear the flag; if fewer than 10, clear it. Needs the mass-mode mission and targets.

I disassembled the cited EXE regions myself with capstone, read the raw bytes of canonical/UFO2P.EXE, and cross-checked with Ghidra decompiles. Every load-bearing step reproduced, and the engine absence is confirmed in both LIVE and HEAD. The corrections above are precision fixes, not a change of verdict. Independent corroboration comes from the web notes, which describe the same "Apocalypse mission": all UFOs except Probes, fired at the next timetabled mission after the Control Chamber falls. Probe is type 0, which the allocator skips. The one place the finding's impact could have collapsed was the stock persistence: if the dimension fleet were wiped when X-COM leaves, the allocation would never reach 10. It is not wiped. FUN_000105b8 runs its sweep of unoccupied cities from the high index down, so the human city is simulated last. When the sweep fires, FUN_00058280 therefore decrements row 0, not row 1, and the dimension stock survives. That matches "UFOs are at 100% health at every visit". - EXE: At most ONE roll set per agent per game midnight. A full-rate agent gets 960 points/day so exactly one set/day; overflow is discarded.

Conversion cadence: FUN_0006c058 sole caller FUN_0006bc70 (file 0xCE314) at 0x6BD96; FUN_0006bc70 sole caller FUN_000105b8 at 0x10842, reached only after SI==0 (0x107A7 JNZ 0x10832 / JMP 0x1080E); FUN_00010908 (day loop, debug string 'in day loop.') listing 0x10969-0x1097F compares weekday [0xD4D64] with entry weekday [0xE131C] and on mismatch jumps to 0x11366 returning AX=[0xE1328] which is cleared at 0x10926; event interrupts set [0xE1328]=1 and return nonzero (exit FUN_000105b8). Cross-check: FUN_0006bc70 also decrements facility build counter cell+0x56 and facility_data build times are days (Training Area 5, file 0x13D804) while the engine decrements buildTime once per day (gamestate.cpp:1567 LIVE / :1534 HEAD). I did not run the game.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: 'Every idle agent goes to Physical/Psi training from day 1' and any stat-growth projection: expect ~1 roll set per agent per day, not 6. Mean-field expected value for a stat starting at 25 after 7/14/28/56 days: EXE 30/35/43/57 vs engine 51/68/86/97.
- FIX SKETCH: In Agent::updateHourly replace the 4-engine-hour tick accumulator with the EXE accrual: while the agent is at its home base, not wounded and assigned, progress += 4*v per game hour (v=min(10,floor(100*F/N))); in Agent::updateDaily (end of day) if progress>=160 run one trainPhysical/trainPsi roll set and set progress=0. Keep the StatGrowthMultiplier cheat on the accrual. Add a test asserting <=1 set per midnight.

Engine side (LIVE and HEAD game/state/shared/agent.cpp are byte-identical):
- Agent::updateHourly (agent.cpp:1105-1127) calls trainPhysical/trainPsi with TICKS_PER_HOUR*100/max(100,usage)*mult. The cheat multiplier defaults to 1.0 (options.cpp:575) and nothing in the harness overrides it.

- The only campaign caller of updateHourly is GameState::updateEndOfHour (gamestate.cpp:1416 and 1543). The skirmish call is the only other one.
- Time steps never exceed 5 game minutes: turbo is TURBO_TICKS and the normal speeds are at most 6 ticks. The hour flag therefore cannot swallow whole hours.
- The 10 starting soldiers are auto-assigned to training at gamestate.cpp:803 (LIVE) and :802 (HEAD), so the path is reachable in every benchmark run.
- Facility build time is decremented once per day at gamestate.cpp:1567 (LIVE) and :1534 (HEAD).

(1) Re-decompiled FUN_000105b8, FUN_00010908, FUN_0006bc70, FUN_0006c058, FUN_0006bf04, FUN_0006bdac, FUN_0004aa7c and FUN_0004ac40 in a private Ghidra copy. (2) The calendar FUN_0004ac40 holds hour, minute, second and tick in the words at 0xD4D68, 0xD4D6A, 0xD4D6C and 0xD4D6E. It increments the weekday word [0xD4D64] only when the hour reaches 24, and wraps it at 7, so the weekday changes once per 24 game hours. (3) FUN_00010908 stores the weekday at entry in [0xE131C]. It leaves its loop only when the weekday differs, or when an event interrupt sets [0xE1328]=1. Otherwise it returns [0xE1328], which is 0. (4) In FUN_000105b8, a nonzero return exits the whole function. A zero return reaches INC [0xD4D64], then CALL FUN_0006bc70, which CALLs FUN_0006c058. The DEC/INC pairs on the weekday around each pass net +1 per outer pass. (5) Callers: a raw E8/E9 byte scan of the code region, independent of Ghidra, matches the Ghidra xrefs. Each of the three functions has exactly one caller: FUN_0006c058 from 0x6BD96, FUN_0006bc70 from 0x10842,

EXE, FUN_0003a910 (bytes at file 0x9CFB4 read directly: 53 51 52 56 57 55 83 ec):
- Role 3 branch, listing 0x3ADDC-0x3AEDE. On the second pass (+0xd4 != 0) it calls FUN_000705f8(target). If the result is not -1 it sets the arrived flag at +0x16a, clears +0xd4, calls FUN_00059148, stores 0 into DAT_000e0cc0, and calls FUN_000ac08c with EDX=5 (call at 0x3AE76). The only exposure-related reads are inside FUN_000705f8, which tests only the record's active byte (+0x15) and the building-id word at +0. If the result is -1, it loops over the species table calling FUN_0005fddc and FUN_0006f7f8.
- Role 2 branch, listing 0x3AFC8-0x3B0A3. FUN_000705a8 returns 1 for an active base whose +0x2BC word is non-zero, or when the override DAT_000e0cc0 is non-zero. If it returns 1, reason 5 follows (call at 0x3B079). If it returns 0, FUN_0007062c sets +0x2BC=1 and nothing else happens.

DAT_000e0cc0:
- Ghidra memory reads 0 at VA 0xE0CC0. As a control, the table at 0xE0CD0 reads the known weights 1,1,3,1,1,3,3,3
- REFUTER (exe): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — Re-derived independently, the BD02 EXE facts hold. Re-decompiling FUN_0003a910 and its helpers, and raw-scanning the executable, shows that role 3 (Infiltration) over an active base calls reason 5 without ever touching the exposure word, and that role 2 (Subversion) is exposure-gated. The "same trigger set" implied by verdict=match is false, though. Overspawn UFOs get +0x15C = 5. That is the role-index row 10 of the table at file 0x18EFF2, read through FUN_0005df1c, and UFO_mission_data records 40-44 carry role 10 for their primaries. The role-5 branch of FUN_0003a910 contains no deposit, no FUN_000705f8 and no call to FUN_000ac08c. The engine routes Overspawn through the infiltration landing, which can start a base defence, so it has a trigger the EXE lacks.
  - CORRECTED: The Infiltration and Subversion halves of BD02 hold, but the verdict "match" does not. The engine's landing path fires a base defence for a wider set of UFOs than the EXE, because it also runs Overspawn UFOs through it. Corrected claim: In UFO2P non-4, FUN_0003a910 role (+0x15C) 3 (Infiltration) over an active base calls FUN_000ac08c(target, 5, UFO type, EBX=1) with no exposure read, no deposit and no 5% roll. Role 2 (Subversion) does so only when FUN_000705a8 returns 1 (exposure word non-zero), otherwise FUN_0007062c marks the base and nothing else happens. No other role starts a base defence from a UFO landing. Role 5 (Overspawn) never reaches reason 5. The engine matches the first two rules, but LIVE gamestate.cpp:1069-1074 and :1158-1162 (HEAD :1068-1073 and :1157-1161) also send Overspawn primaries, and Overspawn incursion attackers, to infiltrateOrSubvertBuilding(subvert=false). That mission picks any alive building within 30 tiles (acquireTargetBuilding), including an X-COM base, then deposits crew and pushes DefendTheBase or calls Base::die. The engine therefore has an extra base-defence trigger from week 7 onward that the original game does not have. Fix: stop routing Overspawn through InfiltrateSubvert and implement the role-5 behaviour (spawn a role-4 craft, no deposit, no base battle). blocked_on_re is true for a faithful replacement, because FUN_00070cc0, FUN_00070a2c and FUN_00070e6c were not decoded. It is false for just suppressing the base-defence trigger.

At Superhuman index weights [1,3,6,10,15,21,28,36,45,55]/220: table B any row Raid 25.0%; Megapol row1 Raid 36.8+Storm 25.0=61.8%; table A row2 29.1+16.4=45.5%; row0 41.4+0. Engine: 10% / 30% / 40%.

EXE side, from raw bytes of canonical/UFO2P.EXE disassembled with capstone (ISO non-4, code file = VA + 0x626A4, data file = VA + 0x616A4):
- The tables are at file 0x188E4C (A) and 0x188E6A (B) and match the finding byte for byte. The code's raw operands 0x577A8 and 0x577C6 map to those offsets.
- FUN_000638dc (file 0xC5F80) sums `count` calls of rand16(max) with max = EAX = 9 and count = EDX = 3. The call site at VA 0x92292-0x9229D loads exactly `mov eax,9; mov edx,3`. The sum is divided by count/2, which is 1 here. It subtracts trunc((4-[word 0xE0C9C])*9/4) and, if the result is below 0 or above 9, restarts all three draws (`jl`/`jg` back to 0x638F2). So it is rejection sampling, not clamping.
- rand16 (VA 0x5D1D8) returns a value in [0..n].
- At Superhuman (offset 0) the index weights are [1,3,6,10,15,21,28,36,45,55]/220. I enumerated them and got the finding's figures for difficulty 4 and the contrast figures for difficulty 0.
- FUN_00092060 stores A's strength at [esp+8] and B's at [esp+0x10]. Row 0 when A<B, row 2 when 2B<A and A!=3, else row 1 (listing 0x922A6-0x922E0). Table A applies when A is 3 or i
- REFUTER (exe): refuted=False verdict_should_be=unchanged — I re-derived the mechanism independently from the UFO2P non-4 binary and found no contrary writer or reader.

1. FUN_000638dc: the listing at VA 0x638DC-0x6394A (file 0xC5F80) is the difficulty-biased draw.
 - It takes 3 draws of rand16(9), inclusive 0..9.
 - It subtracts off = trunc((4-d)*9/4) from their sum. Here d is the high word of the dword at 0xE0C9A, i.e. the word at 0xE0C9C.
 - It rejects and redraws if the result is <0 or >9. The divisor count/2 = 1 for count 3, so it is a plain sum.
2. Difficulty word: five tiny setters at VA 0x62704-0x6273D store 0, 1, 2, 3 and 4 into 0xE0C9C. The difficulty is therefore 0..4, and Superhuman is 4 with off = 0.
3. Scheduler FUN_00092060, with row selection and table choice. I read the listing 0x92060-0x92442 myself.
 - Row 0 if strengthA < strengthB (signed word compare).
 - Row 2 if 2B < A and A != 3 (Megapol).
 - Row 1 otherwise.
 - Table A is used for A in 20..22, A == 3, or org+0x11 != 0 (taken-over flag). Everything else uses table B.
 - Lookup is byte [row*10 + idx + 0x1277a8 (A) or 0x1277c6 (B)], at listing 0x92309-0x92333.
 - Conversion: VA + 0x616A4 gives file 0x188E4C and 0x188E6A. Control: the same mapping gives bytes ce 4b (-

- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — The base-removal rule holds under my own check. Neither the engine description nor the EXE description is wrong. What fails is the finding's fix_sketch 'none' and its strategy rule 'Retreat is not an escape; with two bases the loss is survivable'. The original and the engine diverge on what survives a loss or retreat, and no other finding puts that divergence into the engine-fix plan. F16 files the engine's killing of occupants under 'match', so the gap would fall through. Minor citation error: HEAD's BaseDefense case is battle.cpp:3596-3613, not 3603-3620. LIVE has a 7-line RaidAliens hunk at 3581-3587 that shifts it, so 'LIVE=HEAD' holds for content but not for line numbers. Two edge notes: Battle::winnerHasRetreated is never set (only read at battleview.cpp:2273-2279 and battle.cpp:2884), and TACP has a 'fled... You win' string, so winning and then retreating likely behaves differently in the original. That string is not tied to base defence, so I left it out of the corrected claim.
  - CORRECTED: Split BD14a in two. (1) Base removal on loss or retreat: match, and it holds up. UFO2P non-4 FUN_000b32ac type 2 removes the base unless the success word [0x244084]==1; the engine does the same, and both end the game when the last base goes. (2) What a lost or retreated defence costs: diverges. In the original, FUN_000b3114 kills nobody. It hands the building to the Government, clears that base's event and project rows, and re-homes every employed agent and every X-COM craft whose home was that base to a surviving base, round-robin. Agent write-back after the battle does not depend on the success flag. The briefing's "retreat... to cut your losses" therefore means the squad survives. In the engine, Battle::finishBattle calls Base::die, which runs Agent::die on every agent in building->currentAgents and Vehicle::die on every craft in building->currentVehicles. That includes units that retreated, because retreat never removes them from the building. So an engine retreat loses the base plus every unit and craft in it; the original loses only the base. Fix is not 'none': in the BaseDefense-loss branch (LIVE battle.cpp ~3610-3620), re-home surviving agents and docked craft to a remaining base before Base::die, and apply the same to the empty-base paths (vehiclemission.cpp:2644, organisation.cpp:1299 and :1323). Strategy rule, rewritten: retreat does not save the base. With a second base it saves every unit that leaves the field in the original game, but not in the current engine. 
- REFUTER (exe): refuted=False verdict_should_be=match — I tried to refute on three fronts and found no contrary evidence.

(1) Is the success word what the validator thinks it is? Yes. Both binaries exchange the mission record through files. TACP FUN_00015494 writes scenario/scenarir.dat from TACP 0x1ED294, 0xD4 bytes. UFO2P FUN_000b1d9c loads that file into 0x243FEC, and FUN_00010010 then calls FUN_000b1dc0, which calls FUN_000b32ac. The mapping is record +0x98, which is TACP 0x1ED32C and UFO2P 0x244084. Controls confirm it: +0x38 is mission type in both (0x1ED2CC / 0x244024), the TACP battle total at 0x27A0A4 lands in the 0x24408C argument that b32ac passes to the score routine, and the unit/object/diplo payload sizes 0x2238 / 0x8CA0 / 0x310 are identical on both sides.

(2) What sets the value? TACP FUN_00022f90 zeroes it. It sets 1 when a conscious player-side unit exists and no unit hostile to the player is alive, where hostile means relation <= -50 in either direction (threshold byte 0xCE at TACP 0x2818B8). Both sides alive gives 0. Player side dead with hostiles alive gives -1. The only other 1-writers are TACP FUN_00015494 at 0x157A3 and FUN_00022f90 at 0x23106, and both are gated on [0x1ED2F4]==1 and [0x1ED2CC]==1, meaning miss

Independent re-derivation from UFO2P.EXE (ISO non-4) reproduces every EXE claim: the cutoff compares H = sum of the eight cumulative previous-weeks words (ECX only, never H+W), the relation byte is signed and strict, the cutoff path undoes the old income, zeroes income and sets the latch, and the rollover runs after funding. LIVE gamestate.cpp:1773 and :1792-1801 implement this, every score writer updates totalScore and weekScore in pairs, and nothing clears fundingTerminated. The finding's strategy_rule_affected text, however, is contradicted by its own evidence: because H accumulates all earlier weeks and excludes the current one, the rule is a cumulative-sum-with-one-Monday-lag rule, not "two consecutive bad weeks", and "one bad week cannot end funding" is false for any single week whose net score drives the previous-weeks sum below -2400.   - CORRECTED: Mechanic, EXE reading, LIVE port and verdict (match) are all correct and should NOT be reverted. Only the strategy_rule_affected sentence is wrong and must be replaced. Wrong: "One bad week cannot end funding; two consecutive bad weeks ... or Government relation < -50 at any Monday does". Correct: Let S_k be the sum of the 8 category scores of ALL weeks 1..k (the lifetime total as it stood before the week just ending). At the Monday-midnight assessment of week k+1, funding is cut permanently if S_k < -2400, or if the signed byte relation[Government=2][X-COM=0] < -50. The week being assessed (W) never enters the -2400 test; it only sets the tier adjustment (W < -1600 gives -F/4). Consequences: (1) one very bad week (S < -2400) guarantees the cut ONE Monday later and nothing done in the intervening week can rescue it, because that week's score is not in H until the following rollover (W1=-3000 then W2=+5000: still cut at Monday 2). (2) "two consecutive bad weeks" is neither necessary nor sufficient: W1=-1300, W2=-1300 first cuts at Monday 3, not Monday 2; five weeks of -500 cut at Monday 6 with no single bad week; an early positive bank buffers later losses (+3000 then -5000 gives H=-2000 at Monday 3, no cut). (3) Practical rule: keep the running lifetime score at every Monday midnight at or above -2400 (cumulative, strict < for the cut), and keep Government->X-COM relation >= -50 at every Monday; both are permanent latches (word at VA 0xE0CA0, no clearing writer found; saved/l

### v_market / F-M2 — Week-0 price tiering: what drives the weekly price cut
- impact **high**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence high), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: LIVE economyinfo.cpp:29-61 (HEAD identical): soldThisWeek = max(0, currentStock - lastStock) taken BEFORE decay; price x rand(95..97)% if sold > max/2, x rand(90..95)% if sold > max, x rand(85..95)% if sold > 2*max (note the top tier is a 5-15% cut, mean 10%); clamp [base/2, base]. A one-off dump is penalised once (final price after dumping 200 Toxiguns = 0.899, after 100 Psiclone-class items in the xcom branch 0.899).
- EXE: Price cut driven by the POST-decay standing stock, compared with >=: e=0; if floor(max/2) <= stock e=rand(0..2)+3; if max <= stock e=rand(0..5)+5; if 2*max <= stock e=rand(0..5)+10 (later tier overrides, not cumulative); price=price*(100-e)/100 truncating; clamp [base/2, 2*base]. Never raises a week-0 price. Because stock decays only ~30%/week, standing stock for n sold per week is ~2.3n, so the steady full-price selling rate is ~0.2 x max per week (Psiclone/Elerium ~8/wk; Toxigun max 8 ~1.7/wk, and integer stickiness makes 2/wk already slide to the floor). A dump of 100 Toxiguns reaches the 0.50 floor (0.503 after 26 wk); a dump of 100 Psiclone leaves 0.757.
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE ISO non-4, FUN_0009667c file 0xF8D20 (VA 0x9667C): tier compares at VA 0x96709/0x96727/0x96743 (CMP ... JG skip), rand(2)+3 / rand(5)+5 / rand(5)+10 at 0x96712/0x9672E/0x9674C, IMUL by (100-e) then IDIV 100 at 0x96765-0x96775, clamp 0x9677A-0x9679D. Reads the new stock because FUN_000964d0 runs for all rows first (callers census). Does not read lastStock (+0x12) anywhere in the function.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Sale policy: 'dump in lumps, not on a timer' is wrong against the EXE for every week-0 item. EXE rule: a week-0 price is cut on a Monday tick iff post-decay standing stock >= max/2 (tiers max/2, max, 2*max), the cut is permanent, stock of 1-2 never decays; so pace sales below ~0.2 x max per week per item type, or spread across many item types. Replay (26 wk, EXE vs engine realised/base): Toxigun 4/wk 0.614 vs 1.000; Stasis Bomb 8/wk 0.654 vs 1.000; Disruptor armor torso 8/wk 0.654 vs 1.000; Personal Teleporter 8/wk 0.837 vs 1.000; ≥3 units/wk of a max-40 item (Personal Teleporter at 2.65/wk) is unaffected in both.
- FIX SKETCH: Replace the xcom branch with: price pass reads post-decay stock; e = 0; if (max/2 <= stock) e = randInc(0,2)+3; if (max <= stock) e = randInc(0,5)+5; if (2*max <= stock) e = randInc(0,5)+10; price = trunc(price*(100-e)/100); clamp [base/2, 2*base]. Do not use lastStock/soldThisWeek for the decision.

The manufacturing figures and the new-craft health initialisation also check out. The engine side matches the finding's description.

### v_relations / F05-vehicle-downed — Relation loss when X-COM downs or destroys another organisation's vehicle
- impact **high**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence high), review **contested**, blocked_on_re=False

Called from die() when !crashed (:2026, HEAD :2001), crash() (:2043, HEAD :2018) and startFalling() (:2088, HEAD :2063), so a vehicle can be charged twice.

(a) FUN_00057c8c (vehicle damage applier): the first hit that takes health below the type's crash constitution (not already below): prim(victim org, attacker org, -20), once per vehicle; (b) FUN_00054a28: if the victim slot is freed by this hit (health <= damage, outright destroy) and victim org != firer org: prim(victim,firer,-10) and, if firer != X-COM, prim(firer,victim,+2). A crashed vehicle later finished by a hit gets (b) again. Holder X-COM (X-COM craft lost) is a no-op.

FUN_00054a28 CALL sites file 0xB8141 (-10, imm @0xB8132) and 0xB815A (+2, imm @0xB8151). FUN_000409E0 (crash landing) file 0xA3084.

- FIX SKETCH: Replace the hostility-dependent -5/-30 with: on first crossing below crash health (crash()/startFalling for a vehicle not already crashed): -20 victim->attacker; on destroy-by-hit (die with attacker): -10 and, if attacker org != player, +2 attacker->victim; no hostility test; do not charge in crash() and die() both for the same hit; keep ripple (human city).
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — The finding's engine description is accurate: the LIVE and HEAD line numbers, the -30/-5 hostility split and the three call sites all check out. The EXE description is also accurate in mechanics: the -10/+2 sites and the -20 site exist as cited. The error is applying the -20 to Government and Megapol vehicles. The validator never read the crash-constitution field, which is 0 for every non-UFO vehicle type in the EXE. That makes "EXE costs -20 (crash) or -10 (outright)" and "6 downed Government craft in the EXE" false for the subject mechanic; the right figures are -10 and about 12. The claimed engine double-charge is unreachable for the same reason. Because the strategy rule is built on the 6-versus-4 comparison, the finding as written would feed a wrong number into the engine-fix plan and strategy. The overall verdict (diverges, high impact) does not change.

In UFO2P.EXE ISO non-4, the -20 crash-threshold charge in FUN_00057c8c cannot fire for Government, Megapol, Marsec, civilian or X-COM vehicles. The gate compares post-hit health against the vehicle type's crash constitution (word at VA 0x128618 + type*0x7E, i.e. file 0x189CBC + type*0x7E). That word is nonzero only for the 10 UFO types, 0-9 (15, 35, 150, 150, 200, 200, 100, 80, 300, 350). It is 0 for every non-UFO type, 10-33. After a non-lethal hit health is at least 1, so "health < 0" is never true. The -20 therefore applies only to UFO victims (holder = Aliens). For a Government or Megapol vehicle the EXE charges exactly one thing. When X-COM's projectile frees the vehicle slot (lethal hit, FUN_00057c8c -> FUN_00058280 sets record word +2 = 0xFFFF), FUN_00054a28 calls prim(victim org, firer org, -10) at file 0xB8141. If the firer is not X-COM it also calls prim(firer, victim, +2) at file 0xB815A. Both calls need the firer vehicle record to still be alive and victim org != firer org. There is no hostility test and no -20. The engine charges -30 (or -5 if the victim already considers X-COM Hostile, i.e. < -50) via die(attacker, !crashed). It is therefore 3x harsher than the EXE per Government craft, not 1.5x. - impact **high**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence medium), review **survived**, blocked_on_re=False

- EXE: prim(Alien, X-COM, -20) per UFO downed (crash) and -10 if destroyed outright; the cell m[Alien][X-COM] is already -100 so the effect is the ripple: Government af=trunc(trunc(-50/2)/2)=-12 -> trunc(-12*-20/100)=+2 (+1 outright); Megapol +2/+1; Cult of Sirius (loves Aliens, rel 100) -10/-5.
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE ISO non-4: same FUN_00057c8c site (file 0xBA913, delta -20) and FUN_00054a28 -10 site; ripple arithmetic from FUN_0005faf0 listing (file 0xC2194..); starting matrix at file 0x189040 (28x28 int32; Gov->Alien -50, Megapol->Alien -50, Cult->Alien 100). Reference model validate/_sim_exe_relations.py: dGov=+2/+1, dCult=-10/-5.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Interception strategy: every UFO shot down is worth about +2 Government relation in the original (human city only), roughly 3-4x the engine; use as a partial buffer against relation loss from collateral damage.
- FIX SKETCH: Falls out of F05 (flat -20/-10) plus integer ripple (F01). No extra rule.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=False verdict_should_be=unchanged — I re-read the engine code and the EXE listing independently, and could not break the finding. The engine numbers, the EXE call site and arguments, the ripple arithmetic and the starting matrix all check out. The engine path is reachable by an X-COM interceptor shooting a UFO. The finding's "(human city only)" claim depends on [0xD5060]==0 meaning the human city. I did not re-trace that byte myself. It rests on the validator's and other validators' notes (new-game init clears it, the hourly and daily blocks are gated on it) and on the scan of its accesses.
- REFUTER (exe): refuted=False verdict_should_be=diverges — I re-decompiled the primitive and both call sites in a private Ghidra copy (UFO2P ISO non-4) and re-derived every step independently; nothing contradicts the finding. The primitive FUN_0005faf0 applies the ripple formula exactly as stated. The -20 site is the tail of FUN_00057c8c and the -10 site is in FUN_00054a28's vehicle branch. Both use victim org as holder and firer org as target, with no hostility test. A call-site scan finds exactly 17 callers of the primitive, so no other primitive call fires on a UFO kill. Side note for F05, not a refutation of F06: crash_constitution is 0 for vehicle types 10-32, so the -20 site cannot fire for X-COM or org vehicles, and F05's "downed Gov/Megapol vehicle -20" rows look unreachable. A flat -20/-10 fix inherited from F05 must be limited to UFO types 0-9.

### v_relations / F11-per-kill-rule — Per-kill relation changes in tactical combat (victim org, allies, enemies)
- impact **high**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence high), review **survived**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: LIVE game/state/battle/battleunit.cpp:5075-5101 (HEAD same lines): killer attackerOrg; victim ourOrg->attackerOrg -5 if ourOrg Hostile(<-50) to attacker else -30; then for every org except attacker and Civilian: Hostile(<-50) to ourOrg +2, Allied(>=75) to ourOrg -5. Constants fixed.
- EXE: TACP FUN_00060720 (no killer -> no change). S=byte[victim unit+0x44] (INFERRED agent-type score; humans 10, aliens 3-50). If killerOrg==victimOrg stop. If victimOrg!=27: victim hostile to killer (m<=-50) ? m[v][k]-=S/2 : m[v][k]-=3S (floor -100). For o in 0..27, o!=victimOrg, o!=27: m[o][v]>=75 ? m[o][k]-=S/2 : (m[o][v]<=-50 ? m[o][k]+=S/4 (cap 100)). Matrix is TACP's own copy at VA 0x1D3428, saved back at battle end. Engine constants equal this for S=10 only.
- EVIDENCE: TACP.EXE ISO non-4 (CRC 0xfebbe39e): FUN_00060720 physical 0xBB1C4 (VA 0x60720+0x5AAA4; prologue 5351565755 83ec14 verified); body VA 0x608C3-0x60AB8 (file 0xBB367-0xBB55C); same-org skip VA 0x60966 (0xBB40A); civilian skip VA 0x6096F; helpers FUN_00021998 (file 0x7C43C, hostile m<=byte[0x2818B8]=0xCE at file 0x2D135C) and FUN_000219CC (file 0x7C470, allied m>=byte[0x2818B9]=0x4B). Matrix save FUN_00015494 file 0x6FF38 (size 0x310 at VA 0x1D3428 = file 0x222ECC). Only killer-carrying caller: FUN_0005F860 (file 0xC1F04->0xBB360 region call VA 0x5FCD4); other callers pass killer -1.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Killing aliens raises Government->X-COM by floor(S/4) per kill ONLY when the Government is hostile to the Aliens at the moment: EXE test m[Gov][Alien]<=-50, engine strict <-50; Gov->Alien starts at -50 +/-10 so it is true for about 52% of EXE seeds and 48% of engine seeds (and exactly -50 is hostile in the EXE only). Gains scale with S (Anthropod +2, Skeletoid +3, Psimorph/Megaspawn +7, Queenspawn +12); the engine always gives +2. Killing Government/Megapol units costs 3S=30 (or S/2=5 if already hostile).
- FIX SKETCH: In BattleUnit::die: use S=agent->type->score: direct delta hostile ? -(S/2) : -(3*S) (integer floor); loop over all orgs except victimOrg and Civilian (include attacker as a no-op); allies (>=75) -(S/2); enemies (<=-50) +(S/4); hostile test <=-50, allied >=75; skip the whole block when attackerOrg==ourOrg or no attacker.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=False verdict_should_be=diverges — I re-derived the finding independently from the engine source and a fresh Ghidra decompile of TACP `FUN_00060720` and found no concrete contrary evidence.
- The engine description is exact (same lines in LIVE and HEAD, fixed -5/-30/-5/+2, thresholds `< -50` and `>= 75`, reachable via `applyDamageDirect` -> `die`).
- The EXE listing confirms the rule shape: `S/2`, `3S`, `S/4`, the -100/+100 clamps, the skip conditions, the argument order of both helper calls, and threshold bytes 0xCE and 0x4B.
- S is the agent-type score, traced from UFO2P spawn code through the hand-off record to TACP unit +0x44.
- The matrix persists into the city layer.
The verdict "diverges" is therefore right. What needed fixing was the evidence, not the verdict: one wrong file offset, S being OBSERVED rather than INFERRED, a one-sided strategy sentence, an impact rating inconsistent with the write-up, and a missed killer-remap divergence.
- REFUTER (exe): refuted=False verdict_should_be=unchanged — I re-derived FUN_00060720 from my own Ghidra listing and raw bytes of TACP.EXE (CRC 0xfebbe39e). Every load-bearing claim reproduces, and I found no contrary writer or reader.
- Helper semantics, re-decoded from the listing:
  - FUN_00021998 loads m[a][b] and does CMP AL,AH; JG, with AH = byte[0x2818B8] = 0xCE. So hostile is m <= -50.
  - FUN_000219CC does a signed CMP against byte[0x2818B9] = 0x4B. So allied is m >= 75.
  - Both bytes have only READ xrefs. The control query shows a WRITE for the neighbouring counter at 0x2818BA, so the query would have caught writers.
- Argument order: m[v][k] is victim-to-killer, and the loop reads m[o][v] and writes m[o][k]. This is the same orientation as the engine's code.
- Skip logic: same-org stop at VA 0x60966; Civilian-victim skips the direct term at 0x6096F; the loop runs o = 0..27, skipping o == victimOrg (VA 0x60A02) and o == 27.
- Arithmetic: S/2 is SAR 1; 3S is IMUL AH=3 with a -100 floor store of 0x9C; S/4 is the SBB/SAR 2 idiom with a +100 cap. The seven matrix stores in the function are the only writes (xref census).
- Other callers: FUN_000126d0, FUN_00022020, FUN_0006cf64 and the six sites in FUN_0003d9e4 all do MOV EDX,-1 befor

The unique byte pattern also appears in UFO2P4.EXE (file 0xF9B5A), so the finding holds in both generations. The engine side is confirmed: LIVE organisation.cpp:1240-1246 (signTreatyWith) and diplomatictreatyscreen.cpp are identical to HEAD, and the only call site passes forceAlliance=false, so any myRelation > 0 becomes max(myRelation+25, 100) = 100. ### v_research_rates / RR-09 — Personnel caps (per class and total)

- impact **high**, validator verdict **match** (confidence high), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=False

- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE non-4: calls at file 0x10D5B0 (group 0 branch) and 0x10D950 (group 1 branch) inside FUN_000aac88 (entry file 0x10D32C, VA 0xAAC88); operand 'mov dx, word [ebp+0xd7ee]' (raw 0xD7EE = research_data VA 0xDD7DC + 0x12, data file 0x13EE80 + idx*28 + 18). Table read at file 0x13EE80 (99 records): multiset of (man_hours,score) identical to the engine, alien-building chain records 88-97 identical by position (380,420,320,460,300,440,340,400,360,480).
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Research is the main positive score source (21,010 over all topics). Rank by score per 1000 man-hours: Queenspawn Autopsy 30.0, Anthropod/Hyperworm/Popper/Spitter Autopsy 25.0, Overspawn Autopsy 22.5, Psimorph 20.8, Skeletoid 20.0; the table average is 6.8; Quantum/engineering topics (Dimension Destabiliser 1.2, Personal Cloaking 1.5, Teleporter 1.5, Dimension Shifter 2.1) are the worst.
- FIX SKETCH: none; optionally move the score add out of the UI event handler into the simulation so a headless run cannot drop it.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=engine_bug — The EXE half and the value table hold up under my own check: 99/99 (hours, score, lab group) tuples are identical, and the call sites and operand are as claimed. What fails is the engine half. The finding shows the add sits in a UI event handler but never checks that it fires every time, and the verdict and "fix none" rest only on a code read. The saved games show it does not fire every time.

Cause: the main loop runs processEvents, then update, then applies stage commands (LIVE framework.cpp:539, 548, 558). processEvents sends each event only to the current top stage (line 784). A ResearchCompleted event pushed by an update in the same frame as a queued stage PUSH (a pause notification or a screen the harness opens) reaches that new top stage instead of CityView. CityView::eventOccurred is the only place that credits the score.

The harness already records this failure mode (tools/oa_play.py:2617-2622).

The mechanism is inferred: no save pins which stage received the lost event. The loss itself is observed.

Evidence it happens:
- build-funding/learning-control-seed11/runs/g000-r0002/victory.save has RESEARCH_ALIEN_BUILDING_0 complete (38000/38000, score 380) but totalScore.rese
  - CORRECTED: SCORE-C1 value and cadence match the EXE, but "match / fix none" is wrong for the engine's delivery path. UFO2P.EXE (non-4) credits addScore(1, research_data[idx].score) inside the simulation function, once per topic, in both lab-group branches. The engine credits the same value (99/99 topics, 21,010 total) only inside CityView::handleGameStateEvent (LIVE cityview.cpp:4917-4925, HEAD 4842-4850), after Lab::update pushes the event (research.cpp:508, identical in LIVE and HEAD). The framework dispatches each queued event only to ProgramStages.current() (LIVE framework.cpp:784, HEAD 706). The benchmark's own saves show the credit is sometimes lost: completed topics are missing from totalScore.researchCompleted, short by exactly one topic's score each time. So the engine does not reliably award the EXE's per-completion score. The fix is required, not optional: add the score in Lab::update next to the pushEvent and remove it from the UI handler. The loss rate looks small, so strategy impact is low-to-moderate, not zero. Also correct the strategy text: "table average 6.8" is the pooled rate (21,010 / 3,093,000 man-hours). The mean of per-topic rates is 10.1 and the median is 10.0. "Teleporter 1.5" is the Personal Teleporter (1.54); the vehicle Teleporter module is 2.67.
- REFUTER (exe): refuted=True verdict_should_be=engine_bug — The EXE derivation was re-checked independently and every cited fact reproduces. The verdict changes only because of the engine delivery path, which the validator did not test.

EXE confirmation:
- Prologue at file 0x10D32C is a Watcom push sequence; VA = file - 0x626A4 = 0xAAC88.
- Ghidra xrefs to the research_data score field (VA 0xDD7EE) return exactly 2 reads, at VA 0xAAEFA and 0xAB288. These map to file 0x10D59E and 0x10D92C, both in FUN_000aac88, so no other code reads the score.
- The completed byte [idx*10+0xE2B8] has exactly two writers by raw scan: file 0x10D58C (dh=1) and 0x10D936 (cl=1). Each is a few instructions before its call; the guard `cmp byte [eax+0xE2B8],0; jne` is at 0x10D360.
- Both completion paths end in `jmp 0x10DAAC` (at 0x10D76F and 0x10DA98), which goes to the next topic. The loop is `cmp dx,0x63; jb`, i.e. 99 topics.
- The updater at file 0xFAA80 is `and eax,0xffff; add [eax*8+0x10c5c],edx; ret`. A raw E8/E9 scan finds 9 callers; only 0x10D5B0 and 0x10D950 load eax=1, and the other 7 load categories 5, 5, 6, 6, 2, 0 and 7. An operand census of 0x10C5C..0x10C9B shows no other writer to the category-1 slot (0x10C64/0x10C68).
- The labels at 0x1515B4.. re
  - CORRECTED: The EXE half of SCORE-C1 holds as written. In UFO2P.EXE non-4, FUN_000aac88 (entry file 0x10D32C, Ghidra VA 0xAAC88) calls addScore(1, u16 research_data[idx].score) exactly once per topic, at file 0x10D5B0 (group 0) and 0x10D950 (group 1). The engine's values and once-per-topic rule match: 99 topics, total 21,010, with the 25 engine-only RESEARCH_UNLOCK_* topics scoring 0. The verdict should not be a clean "match", though. The engine credits the score only inside CityView::eventOccurred (LIVE game/ui/tileview/cityview.cpp:4924-4925; HEAD 4849-4850). Lab::update pushes the ResearchCompleted event (research.cpp:508), and Framework delivers events only to ProgramStages.current() (framework.cpp:784). The EXE adds the score unconditionally where it marks the topic complete. In the benchmark saves the engine sometimes completes a topic without crediting it. 2 of 326 scored completions were lost, 460 of 48,790 points, so the effect is small. The "optional" fix is therefore a required, small engine fix: credit the score in the simulation, in Lab::update at completion, or at least in a handler that cannot be missed. Strategy impact of the bug is low.

### v_score / SCORE-C2-TRIGGER — Alien incident trigger probability (how often the -30 and the investigation prompt happen)
- impact **high**, validator verdict **engine_bug** (confidence high), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: LIVE building.cpp:771-840 (HEAD 750-): per attempt each species with count>0 rolls randBoundsExclusive(0,100) < 100/(count+1) (count = aliens of that species), any success marks the building spotted; detectionValue = sum(weight*count) * (function->detectionWeight - 2*difficulty)/100, halved separately for (allied with aliens), (hostile to player) and (taken over); trigger if rand[0,100) < value-10. Attempt cadence TICKS_PER_DETECTION_ATTEMPT[difficulty] = 110 min at Superhuman (building.h:17-19).
- EXE: Every 5 game minutes FUN_0006f964 visits 5 buildings round-robin (109 human buildings in the saved game, so each is visited about every 109 min, independent of difficulty). Per building: for each species with count>0, k = number of species seen so far, roll rand16(100) (inclusive 0..100) < floor(100/k) marks the species; sum += count*alien_detection_weight[species] (egg 1, Brainsucker 1, Multiworm 3, Hyperworm 1, Chrysalis 1, Anthropod 3, Skeletoid 3, Spitter 3, Popper 2, Megaspawn 10, Psimorph 8, Queenspawn 20, Micronoid 1); value = sum*(building_detection_weight - 2*difficulty)/100; ONE halving if relation[org][Alien] >= 75 OR relation[org][X-COM] <= -50, a second halving if the org is taken over; incident if marked and rand16(100) < value-10. Worked example Superhuman, 12 Anthropods in a Corporate HQ: value 43, EXE about 32% per visit versus engine about 2.5%; 3 Anthropods in the Senate: EXE about 3% versus engine about 0.75%.
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE non-4: listing of FUN_0006f964 at file 0xD2008 (species loop VA 0x6F9D2-0x6FA24: inc edi / idiv; weights table via [esi+0xE0CD0] = file 0x142374; building weight table VA 0xF3CB0 = file 0x155354; relation predicates FUN_00091de4 (file 0xF4488) and FUN_00091db0 (file 0xF4454); final roll VA 0x6FB03-0x6FB12). Cadence: FUN_0004ab04 decrements [0xD5068] by 0x2A30 per call (288 calls per 24 h). Saved game buildings.xml: 109 CITYMAP_HUMAN buildings.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Incident frequency drives both score bleed (-30 each) and how soon infiltrating crews are revealed for raids. The engine under-generates incidents for typical crews, so infiltration is detected later and the first-detection/early-game pacing differs from the original.
- FIX SKETCH: In Building::detect: replace 100/(count+1) with 100/k where k counts species with count>0 in iteration order (1-based) and use rand inclusive [0,100]; keep detectionValue as is but halve ONCE if (relation to Aliens >= 75) OR (relation to X-COM <= -50), then once more if takenOver; final test rand inclusive [0,100] < value-10. Use the fixed 5-buildings-per-5-minutes round-robin (or an equal per-building interval of about N_buildings minutes) instead of TICKS_PER_DETECTION_ATTEMPT[difficulty]. Model the first-detection x100 only for the first window if the date words are decoded.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=engine_bug — Independent re-check of the EXE confirms the formula limbs. My own Ghidra listing of FUN_0006f964 (VA 0x6F964, file 0xD2008) shows these. EDI is zeroed at 0x6F9C6 and incremented only at 0x6F9F5, once per species with a non-zero count byte at building+0xD4+s, and the divisor of IDIV at 0x6F9F9 is EDI, not the species count. FUN_0005d1d8 returns the random value mod (n+1), so it is inclusive. At 0x6FA9D to 0x6FAC1 FUN_00091de4(org,1) true skips the second test, and FUN_00091db0(org,0) true halves once, so (A OR B) halves once. The taken-over byte at org+0x11 halves a second time. The final test at 0x6FB08 to 0x6FB12 is a signed JGE after SUB ECX,0xA. The threshold bytes at file 0x188E88/9 are 0xCE (-50) and 0x4B (75). On the engine side, building.cpp:771-840 is correctly described, and the engine is reachable through gamestate.cpp:1456-1531 and cityview.cpp:4840. However, the finding says the EXE cadence is independent of difficulty and recommends replacing TICKS_PER_DETECTION_ATTEMPT with a fixed round-robin. That is refuted by data. The building list is loaded per difficulty, and the engine table is a faithful rounding of those counts. The engine's own comment at building.h:13-15 
  - CORRECTED: The core verdict (engine_bug on the trigger formula) holds, but one EXE sub-claim and part of the fix sketch are wrong. Keep: in UFO2P.EXE non-4 FUN_0006f964 (file 0xD2008), the species roll is rand16(100) < floor(100/k), where k is a counter that is incremented once per species with count>0 (EDI at VA 0x6F9F5, file 0xD2099). The engine uses 100/(count+1), where count is the head count of that species (building.cpp:771-840). The EXE halves ONCE for (relation[org][Aliens] >= 75 OR relation[org][X-COM] <= -50), then once more if the org is taken over. The engine halves up to three times. For a taken-over org the engine therefore divides by up to 8 where the EXE divides by 4. The final roll is signed and inclusive in the EXE (rand16(100) in 0..100, JGE against value-10). It is exclusive in the engine. Correct the numbers: the engine gate uses integer division, so 12 Anthropods in a Corporate HQ at Superhuman gives value 43, with EXE about 32.3% per visit and engine about 2.3% per visit (about 14x, not 2.5% and 13x). 3 Anthropods in the Senate gives EXE 2.9% and engine 0.75%. The ratio is about 2x for 1 alien, about 4x for 3, and about 14x for 12. For multi-species crews it is smaller, about 2.3x for two species of 3 each. Rule re-derivation:

Gating in the projectile routine (FUN_00054a28), read from the listing:
- It runs once per active projectile slot per tick, called from FUN_00054764 over 50 slots.

  - CORRECTED: Mechanic and verdict stand (match). The original game charges Damage to City (cat 6) once per projectile hit on scenery when the current city is the human city, the hit tile's x,y lies in a building's bounding box, the firer vehicle slot is active, and the firer's owner org (vehicle +0x12C) is 0 (X-COM) or 1 (Alien). The charge is -byte[tile type +0x0C], where the tile type is the static 52-byte record at VA 0x153068 + id*0x34, so the value is the pre-hit type. It is charged whether or not anything is destroyed. Writer 2 (UFO2P file 0xA423D, in FUN_00041644) hard-codes org 1 and charges when a crashing vehicle clears building scenery. It is called only for vehicles in mode [v+2]==2 with state [v+0xF6] of 4 or 5, which is the falling/crashing mover. It is not a "ground vehicle" path.

1. Formula, re-derived independently. I re-listed TACP FUN_0001f830 in Ghidra and disassembled the raw bytes of both EXEs with capstone, so I did not rely on the Ghidra constant-folded C. Every stat is `(rec_base + rand_inclusive(rec_inc)) * (100 + 4*D) / 100` with IDIV truncation. TU is `0x1FBE4`, health `0x1FA8D`, stamina `0x1FB58` and reactions `0x1FA43`. UFO2P FUN_000ad93c has the same shape for TU (`0xADAC9`), stamina (`0xADB27`), reactions (`0xADB82`) and health (`0xADDC6`). Both RNG helpers return `x % (n+1)`, so the roll is inclusive, and both preserve registers.
2. Address spaces. TACP 0x1F830 + 0x5AAA4 = file 0x7A2D4 and UFO2P 0xAD93C + 0x626A4 = file 0x10FFE0. Both prologues match the bytes (`5156575583ec1889` and `5156575583ec1489`). The agent_type_data tables (TACP 0x2FFAF8, UFO2P 0x155574, 47x106 bytes) are byte-identical, checked by me.
3. Superhuman numbers. I recomputed them from the raw table bytes and they match the validator's table. Anthropod TU 56->64 and HP 60->69, Skeletoid TU 86->99, Multiworm HP 340->394, Megaspawn HP 260->301, Queenspawn HP 600->696, Psimorph HP 420->487 and Popper TU 110->

### v_takeover / T2 — Takeover cadence, eligible orgs and suppression
- impact **high**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence high), review **survived**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: organisation.h:19 TICKS_PER_TAKEOVER_ATTEMPT = 125 min; each org has its own accumulator with a random initial phase (gamestate.cpp:610-611 HEAD / 611-612 LIVE); Organisation::updateTakeOver organisation.cpp:715-722; driven from GameState::updateEndOfFiveMinutes (HEAD gamestate.cpp:1423-1436 / LIVE 1456-1470), break after first takeover per tick; runs for all orgs regardless of the player's current city. Comment claims the original tested one org per 5 min giving 125 min.
- EXE: One org is tested per 5-minute tick from a 60-slot rotation: counter 2..61 (init 2, wraps when >61), only slots 2..26 are real orgs (Government..Technocrats), so every org is tested once per 300 game-minutes in fixed order, Government at the very first tick of a new game. X-COM (0), Aliens (1) and Civilian (27) are never tested. The whole routine (including the counter advance) is skipped while byte [VA 0xD5060] is non-zero (inferred: player in the alien dimension). Already-taken-over slots are skipped but still consume their tick. Orgs with infiltration speed 0 (S.E.L.F.) can never reach v>100.
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P non-4: counter VA 0xF3DB4 = file 0x155458 (static dword 2); inc at VA 0x70122, wrap 'cmp edx,0x3e / mov [0xF3DB4],2' at VA 0x7012D = file 0xD27BF-0xD27D5; slot<27 test 'cmp ebx,0x1b' at VA 0x6FDD0 (file 0xD2474); early exit 'cmp byte [0xD5060],0' at VA 0x6FDA2 (file 0xD2446). Call chain: FUN_0004ac40 (file 0xAD2E4) minute rollover -> VA 0x4AE82 (file 0xAD526) with eax=minutes word [0xD4D6A] -> FUN_0004aa7c (file 0xAD120) 'minute%5' -> call VA 0x4AACB -> FUN_0004ab04 (file 0xAD1A8) -> call VA 0x4AB31 (file 0xAD1D5). Ghidra ref:f3db4 shows the only writers are in FUN_0006fd9c.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Time-to-loss for an infiltrated org: EXE gives 4.8 attempts/day per org versus 11.5 in the engine; combined with T1 it sets the lead time between 'infestation starts' and 'org lost' (engine median 19-34 h for +1..+2 raw/h, EXE 87-236 h).
- FIX SKETCH: Remove TICKS_PER_TAKEOVER_ATTEMPT, ticksTakeOverAttemptAccumulated and the random init. Add a serialized GameState counter takeoverSlot (init 2); in updateEndOfFiveMinutes, only when the player is in the human city: slot=takeoverSlot; takeoverSlot=(slot+1>=62)?2:slot+1; if (slot<27) find the org with exeOrgIndex==slot (field exists, organisation.h:156) and, if !takenOver, call takeOver(). Update/extend tests (no test currently covers the cadence).
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=False verdict_should_be=diverges — My own disassembly, a fixup-record audit and a check of the second generation all confirm the 300-minute per-org EXE cadence against the engine's 125 minutes. The engine description (line cites, reachability, no test coverage, exeOrgIndex present for all 28 orgs) is also exact. The only inaccuracies are cosmetic.
- REFUTER (exe): refuted=False verdict_should_be=unchanged — I re-derived the cadence independently and found no contrary writer, reader or caller.

Ghidra and a raw byte scan agree on the counter and chain:
- The counter (VA 0xF3DB4, file 0x155458) holds the static value 2.
- Its only code touchpoints, 8 operand hits at file 0xD2455..0xD27D3, are all inside FUN_0006fd9c. No save or load code touches it.
- The wrap is 'cmp edx,0x3e / jl / mov [ctr],2', and the slot gate is 'cmp ebx,0x1b / jge skip'. Both are present in UFO2P4.EXE too.
- Each link of the chain has a single E8 caller: FUN_0006fd9c is called only from FUN_0004ab04, which is called only from FUN_0004aa7c.
- The alternate time path FUN_000114cc rounds minutes up to the next multiple of 5 and fires the 5-minute handler once per jump, so it matches the clock path.
- Taken-over slots skip the roll but still advance the counter.
- S.E.L.F. (index 23) has org-speed 0 in the table at file 0x1424B0, so it can never accrue infiltration.

The engine side also matches the finding. organisation.h:19, organisation.cpp:715-722, gamestate.cpp 611-612 and 1456-1470 (LIVE; HEAD 610-611 and 1423-1436) agree with the finding, and the false '25 orgs x 5 min = 125' comment is at organisation.h:15-18

### v_takeover / T4 — Permanence: infiltration pin, decay, liberation, reversibility
- impact **high**, validator verdict **match** (confidence high), review **contested**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: organisation.cpp:639-642 hourly pins takenOver orgs to 200; takenOver is never reset anywhere; no liberation path; bribescreen.cpp:68-77 refuses.
- EXE: FUN_0006fcc0 re-pins flagged orgs to 200 every hour (no decay). Live writers of the flag byte: new-game init (0), the roll (1), a developer ORGANISATION DATA editor (FUN_00021ddc, field labelled 'like alien'), and FUN_0007abf0, which would take over (killed-weight > alive-weight over the org's records with +0x22>4 in the 180-entry table at VA 0x144918) or LIBERATE (clear flag, M[org][Alien]=-50, M[org][X-COM]=+50) but is UNREFERENCED dead code (only caller FUN_0007acfc has no inbound E8/E9/Jcc/relocated pointer). So there is no live liberation: X-COM cannot reverse a takeover; relations of a taken-over org to X-COM can still receive deltas through the relation primitive but there is no source of +50 per week.
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P non-4: hourly FUN_0006fcc0 file 0xD2364 (cmp byte [esi+eax+0x11],1 at file 0xD2374; mov [ebx+0x6F4C8],0xC8 at file 0xD237B). Flag writers from Ghidra w11 scan + raw scans of every imul ..,0x1B6 / mov eax,[0xAFB4C] neighbourhood: file 0x776BA, 0xD24DF, 0x84B15-ish editor store at VA 0x22671 (FUN_00021ddc file 0x84480, string 'like alien 1%i' file 0x133258), FUN_0007abf0 file 0xDD294 (writes at VA 0x7AC64 / 0x7ACD2), FUN_0007acfc file 0xDD3A0 (call VA 0x7AD26). Controls: live FUN_0007ad3c has about 20 E8 callers; ptr:4A896 finds the jump table at VA 0x4A784. The only raw hit of the value 0x6ACFC (file 0x68DC6) decodes as LE fixup '07 10 B7 07 02 FC AC 06 00' = data object 2, page offset 0x7B7 = operand at VA 0xB47B7 in FUN_000b46c4, not a code pointer (LE header file 0x37F34).
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: A takeover is permanent: prevention is the only strategy; there is no liberation plan.
- FIX SKETCH: none
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=False verdict_should_be=unchanged — I tried to refute T4 on both the engine and EXE sides and could not. The engine side is exactly as stated. In the EXE, no live code clears the flag byte, no code reaches the dead liberation function, and the flag survives save and reload. Permanence matches across non-4 and 4 builds.

Checks that could have flipped the verdict, all negative:
- bulk writes through a call or re-run of the initialiser;
- reload re-zeroing the flag;
- a retail path into the developer editors;
- a second reachable copy of the liberation logic.
- REFUTER (exe): refuted=True verdict_should_be=engine_missing — I re-derived the T4 sub-claims independently and most hold. The strengthened parts are listed under "Confirmed". The refutation rests on one concrete writer the validator missed.

Why it was missed: the validator's flag-writer scans matched only [reg+0x11]. A store to one specific organisation folds the record offset into the displacement: org 24's flag is at 24*0x1B6+0x11 = 0x2921. My scan of every instruction in the code object, for displacements that are k*0x1B6+0x11 or that cover that byte, found exactly one such write, at VA 0xB3359. The same event also writes infiltration[24] = 0, so the T7 statement that no mission result writes infiltration is also false for org 24. That write is the "+1500" entry that my own infiltration-array scan listed and I initially passed over.

Reachability (OBSERVED unless marked):
- FUN_000b32ac is Ghidra function FUN_000b32ac and has a single caller, FUN_000b1dc0, called from the entry/main function at VA 0x102A4.
- The Ghidra decompile of FUN_000b32ac shows the write inside "(DAT_00244024 == 1 || (DAT_000d5060 != 0 && DAT_00244024 == 4))", then "else if (DAT_00244084 == 1)", then "if (DAT_00244048 == 2) {...}".
- FUN_000aff9c, the battle-setup f
  - CORRECTED: Permanence holds for the Government and every organisation except one. Engine and EXE match on all of these: the hourly pin to 200 with no decay, no generic liberation, and bribe refusal. The write-up's universal negative is wrong, though. The unconditional statements are "no live liberation: X-COM cannot reverse a takeover", "FUN_0007abf0 is the only code that can clear the flag", and "no mission result ... writes infiltration". UFO2P.EXE (non-4) has a live, scripted reversal for organisation index 24, the Mutant Alliance, in the post-battle handler FUN_000b32ac (VA 0xB32AC, file 0x115950). On a won battle in the alien-dimension city, when the building function index [0x244048] equals 2, it does the following: (a) writes 0 to the Mutant Alliance's takenOver byte (VA 0xB3359, file 0x1159FD); (b) writes 0 to infiltration[24] (VA 0xB33A0, file 0x115A44); (c) raises M[24][X-COM] to at least 100 and mirrors it into M[X-COM][24]; (d) calls the relation primitive with -50 and then clamps M[24][Aliens] to -100. The engine has no equivalent (takenOver is only ever set true, and the Mutant Alliance conversion is not modelled), so this is an engine_missing divergence of low impact. The generic liberation function FUN_0007abf0 is dead code in both UFO2P generations. The strategy rule "a Government takeover is permanent; prevention is the only strategy" is unaffected.

  - CORRECTED: The divergence is real, but the finding states its direction backwards. UFO2P.EXE (non-4) stops weekly UFO growth when alien building function-index 7 (Control Chamber, name-table order) is destroyed. The engine stops it only when BUILDINGFUNCTION_ORGANIC_FACTORY (function-index 5) is dead. In the forced linear raid sequence, which both the engine (campaign-plan §10.2, extract_buildings.cpp:82-86) and the validator's own note place the Control Chamber at ("UFOpaedia numbers the Control Chamber 6th"), the Control Chamber is the 6th raid (file index 5) and the Organic Factory is the 8th (file index 7). The original therefore cuts growth TWO RAIDS EARLIER than the engine, and the engine keeps reinforcing UFOs two raids LONGER. The engine is the harsher case, not the more lenient one. The finding's "index 7 = two raids later; engine cuts growth two raids earlier" mixes the name-order index (Org Factory 5, Control Chamber 7) with raid order (Control Chamber 5, Org Factory 7). The strategy rule to correct: in the original, raiding the 6th building (Control Chamber) ends growth, and the same event arms the F5 mass-launch. The campaign-plan 3.7 lever "raid the Organic Factory (#7) to stop reinforcement" is therefore both wrong for the original and needlessly late. Fix sketch: gate on a latched "Control Chamber destroyed" flag. Doing so makes the benchmark easier, not harder, from the 6th raid on.

  - CORRECTED: Verdict stays "diverges", but the claim as written is wrong on direction and mechanism. (1) In the original, weekly UFO growth stops permanently when the Control Chamber (alien-building function word 7) is destroyed. The Control Chamber is raid #6 in campaign order. The engine instead stops growth when the Organic Factory (function word 5) dies, and that is raid #8. So the engine cut-off comes two raids LATER than the EXE's, and the engine keeps growing UFOs through raids 7 and 8 when the original would not. The finding states the opposite ("original two raids later, engine two raids earlier"). It also calls the Organic Factory "6th/index 5", but it is the 8th raid; index 5 is only its position in the name table. Strategy rule: "raid the Organic Factory to stop UFO reinforcement" is wrong against the original, because growth is already off after raid 6. Raid 6 is also the event that arms the F5 mass launch. (2) The nine bytes written by FUN_000b4538 are not per-building "destroyed" flags. Each is byte0 of a 10-byte research-status record (array base Ghidra VA 0xDE2B0 = file 0x13F954, record r at 0xDE2B0 + 10r), and records 88-97 are the ten alien-building research topics. Destroying building f sets the "force-available" byte (tested by FUN_000aa7a8 at VA 0xAAA8E) of the NEXT building's topic in the raid chain. DAT_000de62a is record 89, the Spawning Chamber topic, so it is set when the Control Chamber falls. DAT_000de63e (case 5) is record 91, the Megapod topic, not an Organi

- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — VC-1 rests on two things: UFO2P raises victory when the f=9 raid is a success, and the success flag is "inferred" to mean the engine's playerWon. The validator's own open question 4 left the TACP producer of that flag unread. I read it, and it contradicts the equivalence.

1. The flag is one shared field. TACP saves a 0xD4-byte block from 0x1ED294 as scenarir.dat. UFO2P loads the same file at 0x243FEC (FUN_000b1d9c). Flag 0x1ED32C in TACP is 0x244084 in UFO2P, both at block offset +0x98.
2. Block offsets cross-check: mission type is 0x1ED2CC = 0x244024; the building function index f is 0x1ED2F0 = 0x244048; the building owner is 0x1ED2F4 = 0x24404C.
3. Only two TACP functions write the flag: FUN_00022f90 and FUN_00015494.
4. For owner==1 and type==1 (alien-building raids), both functions gate success on FUN_00025310(), which returns 1 only when no map tile matches the building's objective-tile row. Row f=9 is {76,77,78,138}; the row-9 offset is quoted in the evidence.
5. Alien-dimension raids are type 1: the launch call at VA 0x7185B passes kind 0 when [0xD5060]!=0. FUN_000aff9c maps every kind other than 5, 3 and 1 to type 1.
6. The engine's win test is Battle::checkMissionEnd, LIV

1. The guard is real. Raw bytes at file 0xD1450 are `53 51 52 56 57 | 80 3d 2a e6 00 00 00 | 75 57`. That is push x5, `CMP byte [0xDE62A],0` (raw operand 0xE62A + 0xD0000), `JNZ +0x57`. The JNZ target is file 0xD14B5, which holds `5f 5e 5a 59 5b c3` (pop edi, esi, edx, ecx, ebx; ret), so a non-zero flag skips the whole function. A Ghidra listing of FUN_0006edac (VA 0x6EDAC) shows the same, and the body only adds table rows to the stock at 0x143D40[type] and clamps to caps.

2. The case-7 write is real. I read the jump table at file 0x116BB4 directly: 10 entries 0xA4545 + 8n, each `c6 05 <addr> 01 c3`. Case 7 at file 0x116C21 is `mov byte [0xDE62A],1`. Case 5 (the Organic Factory) at file 0x116C11 is `mov byte [0xDE63E],1`.

3. The argument is the function index. FUN_000b32ac passes `[0x244048]` in EAX to FUN_000b4538 (VA 0xB33BE). `[0x244048]` is stored at VA 0xB00AB (file 0x11274F) as `dword[building+0xA8] >> 16`, with no offset adjustment.

4. No contrary writer exists. I scanned every instruction in the program for operands in [0xDE280,0xDE720), 241 hits. The only fixed-address writes to 0

- impact **high**, validator verdict **engine_missing** (confidence high), review **survived**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: No such rule. GameLost is raised only by Base::die when player_bases is empty (LIVE:game/state/city/base.cpp:155-159); no balance test in gamestate.cpp.
- EXE: At the very end of the weekly funding function, after salaries, maintenance and the balance commit, the EXE calls FUN_00060ea4(1). It ends the game ('X-COM IS DEFEATED' with the bad-debts text) if X-COM balance (org0+8, signed) < -5000.
- EVIDENCE: FUN_00060ea4 file 0xC3548; CMP dword ptr [EAX+8],0xFFFFEC78 at file 0xC3557. Call site VA 0x94A52 (file 0xF70F6) in FUN_000941dc with EAX=1, after the commit MOV [EDX+8],EAX at file 0xF70DD. Defeat text group 0x2C items 0x14 (bad debts) and 0x15 (all bases destroyed).
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Hard floor: the balance must not be below -5,000 after any Monday's payroll and upkeep. Spending plans that rely on running a deficit are fatal in the original.
- FIX SKETCH: After weeklyPlayerUpdate payroll and maintenance, raise GameLost if player->balance < -5000.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=False verdict_should_be=engine_missing — I could not refute it on the EXE side or the engine side, and I checked both independently.

EXE side:
- The raw bytes of canonical/UFO2P.EXE (CRC 0x4749ffc1) at file 0xC3557 are `81 78 08 78 ec ff ff 7d 3c`. That is CMP dword [EAX+8],-5000 followed by a signed JGE, so defeat is strictly below -5000 and exactly -5000 survives.
- The call at file 0xF70F6 (`b8 01000000 e8 4dc4fcff`) resolves by hand to file 0xC3548, so the call target does not depend on Ghidra.
- FUN_000941dc has exactly one RET (0xF7104), and nothing jumps into 0x94A4D-0x94A60. The check therefore runs on every call.
- DAT_001A892C has a single writer (file 0xFB7C1, FUN_00098a70). It stores (org0+8 - salaries) - maintenance with no clamp, and 0xF70DD commits it to org0+8. The two functions between commit and check (FUN_00050b10, FUN_0004e1b4) only reset UI state.
- Mode 1 selects text group 0x2C item 0x14 in FUN_00060f4c, and the string at file 0x153F9A reads "Due to bad debts...". Mode 2 selects item 0x15 (base loss). The screen waits for input and then runs the shutdown and exit chain.

With EDX nonzero it calls FUN_00060f4c, then FUN_000102fc, then FUN_000befb1. (b) FUN_00060f4c shows "X-COM IS DEFEATED" (group 0x2C item 0x11). Argument 1 selects item 0x14, which I read from the EXE as "Due to bad debts the X-COM organization has been closed down...". Argument 2 selects item 0x15, "All X-COM bases have been destroyed...". The 16-slot base scan in FUN_00060ea4 uses EDX=2. (c) The call site in FUN_000941dc is MOV EAX,1 at VA 0x94A4D (file 0xF70F1) and CALL 0x60EA4 at VA 0x94A52 (file 0xF70F6). ### v_agents / V-AG-02 — Training facility capacity and efficiency (usage > 100%)
- impact **medium**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence high), review **survived**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: LIVE==HEAD agent.cpp:1108-1122 and base.cpp:535-545,649-662: usage = trainees with this home / capacity (10 per Training Area / Psi-gym); usage=max(100,usage); progress rate scales 100/usage, i.e. linear slowdown above 10 trainees per facility.
- EXE: v = min(10, floor(cap*10*F/N)) per accrual with cap=10 for both facilities (F = finished facility cells in the base, N = trainees of that kind in the base); a set needs A>=160, A grows 96*v per day at home. Hence full rate (1 set/day) holds for N<=10F, and one set/day is still obtained up to N<=50F (v>=2) if the agent is at the base >= ceil(160/(4v)) hours that day (v=10:4h, 5:8h, 2:20h); v=1 gives one set every 2 days; N>100F gives v=0. The agent-screen 'efficiency=' shows min(100, 10*F*100/N).
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P non-4. FUN_0006bf04 file 0xCE5A8 (decompile + listing 0x6BF04-0x6C057): terms DAT_000dc168>>16 (Training Area) file 0x13D80E = 10, DAT_000dc178>>16 (Psi-gym) file 0x13D81E = 10, DAT_000dc158>>16 (Medical Bay) file 0x13D7FE = 10 (data file = VA+0x616A4; these are facility_data +0xA capacity words). Counts built by FUN_0006bdac file 0xCE450 (only cells with cell[+0x56]==0 are counted, so facilities under construction do not count). UI formula: FUN_0004f290 file 0xB1934 listing 0x4F5E3-0x4F6E3 (EAX=word*count*100/trainees, cap 100) and strings Combat/Psionic training (efficiency=, No training facilities in base, No Psi-gyms in base resolved by rscan at 0x4F6F8/0x4F656/0x4F6C0/0x4F61E.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Facility sizing: 'one Training Area per 10 trainees'. The EXE does not slow until ~50 trainees per facility (given enough hours at base), so one Training Area plus one Psi-gym covers a whole roster in the original; in the engine each overload step directly reduces gains.
- FIX SKETCH: Use the EXE accrual (see V-AG-01): v=min(10, floor(100*F/N)) with F = finished facilities of that type at the home base and N = trainees of that kind at the base; drop the 100/usage tick scaling. Show the efficiency label unchanged.

Assignment byte +0xC8 is 0 at creation (FUN_000791e0 writes it at 0x795A1), is not touched by the hire commit FUN_0007ea7c, and is written nonzero only by the two UI handlers FUN_00051D0C/FUN_00051D98 (stores at 0x51D5F/0x51DF4). No auto-assignment of the 10 starting soldiers was found (inferred).

Negative rests on the operand scan and the creation routine, not on a full decompile of FUN_000799dc/FUN_000b94c0.
