- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — The validator's negative ("no auto-assignment writer for the starting soldiers") rests on a [reg+0xC8] operand scan. That scan cannot see the static-base form `[EDX+0x1449E0]` used by FUN_000799dc, which the validator admitted it had not decompiled. Decompiling it and scanning every person-table reference with (disp-0x144918) mod 0xCE == 0xC8 shows six real stores of 2, one per group of starting soldiers. So "EXE has no auto-assignment", "starting soldiers are not training until the player assigns them", and the fix of removing the initial assignment are all wrong. The engine's Psi branch never fires (human psi_energy range 5..20), so the effective default matches the EXE. The wounded-trainee gate in FUN_0006bf04 was independently confirmed, and the narrowed divergence also covers the trainee count (FUN_0006bdac vs base.cpp). Engine descriptions (agent.cpp:402-409, :1105-1127, gamestate.cpp:802/803) are accurate and reachable.
  - CORRECTED: V-AG-05 is narrowed, not wholly wrong. (1) Default assignment is a MATCH in effect, not a divergence. UFO2P.EXE (ISO non-4) new-game generator FUN_000799dc (VA 0x799DC, file 0xDC080) DOES auto-assign the 10 starting soldiers: six stores `MOV byte [EDX+0x1449E0],CH|BL` (CH/BL = 2 = Combat training) at file 0xDC116, 0xDC2BB, 0xDC460, 0xDC5DA, 0xDC745, 0xDC860, covering 2+2+2+2+1+1 = 10 agents. 0x1449E0 - 0x144918 = 0xC8, the assignment byte. The 15 staff (5+5+5) are not assigned. The engine assigns Psi if initial psi_energy > 30 else Physical (gamestate.cpp:802 HEAD / :803 LIVE), but the only game-start soldier type (AGENTTYPE_X-COM_AGENT_HUMAN) has psi_energy 5..20, so all 10 start Physical. Benchmark saves confirm 10/10 Physical. Hire leaves the assignment unassigned in both (EXE FUN_000791e0 writes 0 at 0x795A1; engine agent.cpp:402-409). (2) The injured-trainee divergence stands, with one extra part. EXE FUN_0006bf04 (file 0xCE5A8) sends wounded agents (cur +0x3C < max +0x3B) down the heal branch only, so they never accrue training. EXE FUN_0006bdac also leaves wounded agents out of the trainee count N. The engine (agent.cpp:1105-1127) trains injured agents, and base.cpp:535-554 counts them toward Physical/Psi usage. Strategy-rule correction: the starting soldiers already train combat from day 0 in the original; the harness must not press BUTTON_AGENT_PHYSICAL on them, because it toggles training off. Fix sketch: gate trainPhysical/trainPsi on health AND exclude wounded age

### v_agents / V-AG-14 — TACDATA/EXPERIEN.DAT: format and consumer
- impact **medium**, validator verdict **engine_missing** (confidence medium), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=True

- EXE: EXPERIEN.DAT is an AI sample log: 114-byte (0x72) records, first u16 = 2, then 28 int32; appended by the battle loop (FUN_000A12A4 writes ready records from a 100-entry ring at VA 0x276BD4 and sets DAT_0027985C=1), merged from TMPEXP.DAT at the debriefing (FUN_000A1324) and processed by float routines FUN_000A15F4/FUN_0009F2B0; WEAPEXP.DAT (17,160 u16, default 0x32) is loaded at battle start (FUN_00014948). The path is live in single-player (guard flags are a command-line option and a network mode flag). The soldier stat award is a different function (FUN_000b3f74).
- EVIDENCE: TACP non-4: FUN_000a12a4 file 0xFBD48 (listing 0xA12A4-0xA1322, open flags 0x231, record size 0x72, flag byte 2); FUN_000a1324 file 0xFBDC8; FUN_000b3b28 file 0x10E5CC with rscan pair g35/i17 'Experience Processing' at call 0xB3C07; flags DAT_0027A805 (set at 0x1044B in FUN_00010294 from argv), DAT_001B7BAC (writers 0x6FEEE=1, 0x7054B/0x705D5=2). Data: depot_7661 EXPERIEN.DAT 899,346 B = 7,889 x 114 (played/seeded Steam copy, so the count is not part of the format); WEAPEXP.DAT 34,320 B. UFO2P strings WEAPEXP0/BRAIN0.DAT at file 0x1349B8/0x1349D0.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Do not treat EXPERIEN.DAT as an XP table. The original carries learned AI state between battles (written every battle); alien behaviour may adapt over a campaign, unquantified. Contradicts docs/campaign-plan.md section 16.1.
- FIX SKETCH: Docs: correct campaign-plan section 16.1 and the processExperience comment (the soldier award is FUN_000b3f74). Engine: no change until the WEAPEXP/BRAIN consumers in the alien decision code are decoded.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=engine_missing — The verdict class (engine_missing) holds, and my own decompiles strengthen the core claim. The sample ring, its writers and finalisers, the in-battle flush, a BRAIN.DAT network evaluator called from the alien AI step, and UFO2P new-game/save-restore references to the three files are all observed. But the finding states several concrete facts that my independent check contradicts. (1) FUN_000a1324 is described as a merge from TMPEXP.DAT. The listing shows a rename-and-trim to the newest ~10,000 records. (2) WEAPEXP.DAT is called 17,160 u16. The loader, init loops and file contents show two 0x4308-byte tables. (3) FUN_0009f2b0 is called an EXPERIEN processor. It is a decision-time feature routine, and the actual consumer FUN_000a0fb0 is located, which the finding said was undecoded. (4) The 'engine comment' and 'NOT extracted' do not exist in engine code. They come from a docs table row, so the C++ comment fix does not exist. (5) The flush runs inside the time-gated AI step, not only at debrief. Because the written EXE description and the fix sketch are inaccurate, I mark the finding refuted with the same verdict class and give the corrected statement. The strategy rule still holds: 
  - CORRECTED: VERDICT CLASS UNCHANGED (engine_missing, blocked_on_re narrower than stated). The core claim survives my independent re-decompile: EXPERIEN.DAT is not an XP table, it is the sample log of a learning AI that the engine does not have. Several stated facts are wrong, and the fix is docs-only. Corrected finding:

EXE (TACP non-4 unless noted; file = VA+0x5AAA4, prologue opcodes checked against canonical/TACP.EXE):
1. Record: 0x72 bytes = {u8 state, u8 pad, i32 id, i32 n, i32 feat[n<=25], i32 result at +0x6E}. FUN_000a1148 (file 0xFBBEC) claims a slot in the 100-slot ring at VA 0x276BD4 and sets state 1. FUN_000a124c (0xFBCF0) sets state 2 and stores the result. FUN_000a1234 (0xFBCD8) discards. FUN_000a12a4 (0xFBD48) appends state-2 slots to EXPERIEN.DAT (open flags 0x231), clears the state byte and sets DAT_0027985C. Depot record 0: id 101, n 25.
2. FUN_000a1148 has six callers (0x907a4, 0x98b54, 0x99a35, 0x9a45f, 0x9b0c5, 0x9bd2a) in the AI decision functions. FUN_000a124c has two callers (FUN_00092354, FUN_00096908).
3. The flush runs inside the battle AI step FUN_00095490 (file 0xEFF34). It is time-gated: when DAT_002a1760 < the battle clock it calls FUN_000a12a4, then FUN_0009ec78 (0xF971C, writes WEAPEXP.DAT) if DAT_001B7BAC==0, then sets the next time to now+500. It is not a debriefing-only path.
4. ERRATUM: FUN_000a1324 (0xFBDC8) is NOT a merge from TMPEXP.DAT. It is a rotation that caps the log. Count = findfirst size / 0x72. If count <= 10000 (CMP ECX,0x2710 at 0xA1372)

Confirmed:

- The cell type 
  - CORRECTED: BD04 verdict (diverges) stands and the roster facts are correct. Two parts of the finding need correcting: the "cap active stations at 3" fix item, and `blocked_on_re: false`.

Corrected EXE behaviour.
- The roster builder `FUN_000ac7d0` selects present agents of classes 0..3 (any armament) and sets the agent budget to `36 - min(12, 4 * finished 0x17/0x18 cells)`. If the present total exceeds the budget, each class count is scaled to `floor(count * budget / total)`, and the first n agents of each class in array order are taken. `FUN_000acbb4` also stops at 36 X-COM units and 60 units in total. The turret count is added to the unit total only.
- The "12" is a UFO2P reservation clamp. It is not evidence that the original only activates 3 Security Stations.
- UFO2P map assembly `FUN_000b38b4` loads the sector of every finished cell with no station counter, rand16 call or compare against 3 or 12 (also checked in `FUN_000b3c90`, `FUN_000b1000` and `FUN_000b14f0`).
- TACP then spawns one turret unit per map feature byte 0xE6..0xED (230..237), with no clamp inside that function. Each spawn goes through the generic 60-slot unit allocator, which returns -1 when no slot is free. The number of turrets that actually spawn with 4 or more stations therefore depends on how many unit slots are still free when TACP runs that spawn.
- Which of the 60 slots are already taken depends on whether the roster units load before the turret spawn at call site VA 0x107d6 in TACP `FUN_00010294`. I did no

- ENGINE: LIVE economyinfo.cpp:32-41: lastStock=currentStock; rnd=rand[0,100): <30 -> stock=lround(0.80*last), <60 -> lround(0.66*last), else unchanged (40%). Expected multiplier 0.838. No integer stickiness (rounds, so 1 -> 1, 2 -> 2 at x0.8 rounds to 2/1).
- EXE: If stock!=0 and min==0: stock -= stock/8 (always), r=rand(0..100) inclusive: if r>40 stock -= stock/5 (60/101), then if r>70 stock -= stock/3 (30/101), cumulative; all integer truncation. Expected multiplier on a large stock 0.406*0.875 + 0.297*0.70 + 0.297*0.467 = 0.70. Stocks under 8 take no /8 bite; 1-2 are immortal; 3-4 shrink only through the /3 term.
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE ISO non-4, FUN_00096538 file 0xF8BDC (VA 0x96538): listing 0x965AC-0x9660D: CMP [EBX+0xE],0 ; JZ ; CMP word [EBX+6],0 ; JNZ ; SAR/SHL/SBB/SAR 3 (stock/8 signed), rand(0x64), CMP AX,0x28 ; IDIV 5, CMP SI,0x46 ; IDIV 3. FUN_0005d1d8 file 0xBF87C returns value % (n+1), i.e. inclusive 0..n.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Standing-stock half-life: how fast a price slide stops. EXE equilibrium stock ~2.3 x weekly sales vs ~5 x in the engine's decay; EXE small-stock stickiness means max<=8 goods stay above max/2 once 4 units are in the market. Use together with F-M2 for any weekly-sales cap.
- FIX SKETCH: Implement the three integer steps exactly (stock -= stock/8; r=randInc(0,100); if r>40 stock -= stock/5; if r>70 stock -= stock/3) and apply to every row with weekAvailable==0 (not only manufacturer==X-COM).

EXE:
- At 0x965AC the code tests stock != 0 and word +6 (min) == 0, then does stock -= stock/8 (signed Watcom idiom).
- It then calls rand(100), which is inclusive 0..100 because FUN_0005d1d8 takes the value mod (n+1).
- If r > 40 (CMP AX,0x28 ; JLE skips) it does stock -= stock/5. If r > 70 (CMP SI,0x46 ; JLE) it does stock -= stock/3 on the already-reduced stock.
- Word +6 is min. The clamp code in the same function uses +8 as max and +6 as min, and the table parse shows all 135 week-0 rows have min 0.

- impact **medium**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence medium), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=True
- ENGINE: LIVE battlemap.cpp:1542-1543 sets state.getPlayer()->current_relations[target_organisation] = -100 (X-COM's row only), which Organisation::adjustRelationTo (organisation.cpp:1056-1076, same in HEAD) re-mirrors from each org's own view at the next relation change involving X-COM, so it is transient. buildingscreen.cpp:248 applies owner->adjustRelationTo(X-COM,-200) only under mod RaidHostileAction (default off, framework/options.cpp:560). - EXE: For launcher type 3 (building raid): owner = byte[building+0xC8]; if rel[X-COM][owner] (byte 0x16EC28+owner, matrix row 0) > -60 it is set to -60 (0xC4) and the parallel byte at 0x16F078+owner is also set to -60. Type 4 does the same to the other direction rel[org][X-COM] for an organisation passed in a register. Both matrices (0x16EC28, 0x16F078, 0x16F388) are written by the savegame routine, so this is persistent. INFERRED consequence: FUN_00091db0 has 34 call sites (loot gate, buy block, guard-count FUN_000aec70, projectile/obstruction/combat code FUN_00054a28 x4, FUN_00041644 x2, FUN_0004e48c x2, FUN_00058b88 x3, ...), so the persistent X-COM->org <= -60 changes X-COM-side hostility decisions in combat code (arguments not decoded except the loot gate and the buy block). It does NOT by itself block X-COM buying from the org (the buy block reads rel[manufacturer][X-COM], F-M5).
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE ISO non-4: FUN_000ac348 file 0x10E9EC (VA 0xAC348); type-3 branch listing VA 0xAC5A4-0xAC5DB: MOV AL,[EBX+0xC8]; MOV EDX,[EAX+0x16EC25]; SAR EDX,0x18; CMP EDX,-0x3C ; JLE ; MOV [EAX+0x16EC28],0xC4 ; MOV [EAX+0x16F078],0xC4 (file 0x10EC6A compare, 0x10EC71/0x10EC7F writes); type-4 branch VA 0xAC53B-0xAC577 (file 0x10EC0E-0x10EC1B). Save routine FUN_000a3234 file 0xA58D8 writes the three 0x310-byte matrices (listing shows MOV EAX,0x16EC28 / 0x16F388 / 0x16F078, EDX=0x310). Mapping of engine RaidHumans vs Exterminate to launcher types 3/4 not established (FUN_000ac08c file 0x10E730 turns a vehicle-order byte 3 into type 4).
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Raiding a FRIENDLY organisation for loot (Government/Megapol/corporations: Elerium C-spots 3,000 each): in the EXE X-COM's view of it goes to <= -60 for the rest of the campaign (effect on X-COM's combat hostility tests, not on purchases); in the engine nothing persists. Raiding the already-hostile Cult/Psyke group has no extra cost in either. Do not rely on the engine's forgiveness when planning raids on friendly organisations.
- FIX SKETCH: The engine's X-COM row is a mirror of every org's view (organisation.cpp:1066-1076 rewrites it on every adjustRelationTo involving X-COM), so writing -60 at launch is a no-op unless the mirror is removed for that cell. Needed: a directed X-COM row (or separate xcomView[org] cell, plus long_term copy) that the mirror does not overwrite, consulted by the hostility tests the EXE routes through rel[0][x]; at battle creation do xcomView[target] = min(xcomView[target], -60) persistently. Do not just set current_relations at battlemap.cpp:1542.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — The finding infers persistence only from the fact that the savegame routine writes the matrices. It never checks the EXE's X-COM-row mirror, FUN_0005faa8, which rewrites the cell the type-3 branch writes. The sibling validator v_relations had already found this mirror and flagged the -60 as transient (v_relations.md:185). I re-derived it independently in UFO2P.EXE ISO non-4. Code file offset = VA + 0x626A4. 0x5FAA8 gives 0xC214C, which matches the v_relations receipt.

Sequence for a player-launched building raid:
1. The "Raid" handler at VA 0x71820 passes EDX=3 to FUN_000ac08c (call at 0x7186B) when the owner is not X-COM and [0xD5060]==0. FUN_000ac08c calls FUN_000ac348 at 0xAC2F7.
2. FUN_000ac348's type-3 branch stores -60 into m[0][owner] and the shadow (0xAC5CD, 0xAC5DB). The loot gate FUN_00091db0(0,owner) follows at 0xAC73E, then state [0xD4CBC]:=4 and FUN_000a3234 (the save, 0xAC783).
3. FUN_000aff9c maps launcher type 3 to descriptor kind 4 ([0x244024]:=4 at 0xB0128). FUN_000b32ac handles kinds 1 and 4.
4. On return, FUN_00010010 calls FUN_000b1dc0 at 0x102A4. That calls FUN_000a4054 (load) at 0xB1DCB, then FUN_000b32ac unconditionally at 0xB1E79. FUN_000b1dc0 is the only 
  - CORRECTED: The central claim is wrong: the EXE's raid-launch write is NOT persistent for the rest of the campaign. In UFO2P.EXE (ISO non-4) launcher type 3 writes -60 into X-COM's own row cell m[0][owner] (and the shadow at 0x16F078+owner). That row is a mirror that the EXE rebuilds from the organisations' own views (m[0][j] := m[j][0], FUN_0005faa8) on every return from tactical, so the -60 is wiped right after the battle. The engine is transient across the battle boundary too (Battle::exitBattle restores the row, battle.cpp:3199-3208, same line in LIVE and HEAD). The real residual divergence is inside the battle only, and is LOW impact. In the EXE, X-COM's row for the raided org stays <= -60 for the whole tactical session, because TACP has no mirror. In the engine, Organisation::adjustRelationTo re-mirrors on any X-COM-involved change, "even mid-battle" (organisation.cpp:1060-1076), and the first X-COM kill triggers it (battleunit.cpp:5081/5086/5095/5099). After that, X-COM's row for the raided org reverts to that org's view of X-COM. For a friendly org this changes player->isRelatedTo(org) checks in battle: the mission-end hostile-org test (battle.cpp:2248), kill-score credit (battleunit.cpp:5110) and battleview targeting. Two further corrections. (a) The type-4 branch is the only persistent launcher effect: it writes m[org][0] (column 0), which the mirror never rewrites. The org index comes from the stack at [ESP+0xC], not from a register. It is org/event-initiated, per v_relations

### v_research_rates / RR-10 — Daily hire-pool arrivals and churn
- impact **medium**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence medium), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: updateHirableAgents: per hiring organisation, 'attempts' rolls with chance 25+relation (or (50+relation)/2 below 0), none for hostile orgs; 33% of every hirable agent leaves daily (CHANGE_HIREE_GONE, organisation.h:22). Arithmetic from organisations.xml: ~10 attempts/class/day => ~2.5-4.5 arrivals per class per day at relation 0-20 (INFERRED).
- EXE: FUN_0007a474 daily: per class applicants thinned by rand16(0..count/3+1); then n = min(rand16(0..3), cap-(employed+remaining)) new applicants (origin org code rand16(0..0x14)+2, not relation-weighted); agents class additionally rand16(0..1) Hybrids if relation[Mutant Alliance][X-COM] (VA 0x16EEC8) >= 0 and rand16(0..1) Androids if relation[S.E.L.F.][X-COM] (VA 0x16EEAC) >= 0. Mean 1.5 arrivals per technical class per day; equilibrium pool ~6.
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE ISO non-4: FUN_0007a474 file 0xDCB18 (Ghidra VA 0x7A474, delta +0x626A4); removal roll file 0xDCB81-0xDCB9A; arrival roll 'MOV EAX,3; CALL FUN_0005d1d8' file 0xDCC3D-0xDCC48; hybrid/android relation compares at file 0xDCCCE and 0xDCD4B (VA 0x16EEC8/0x16EEAC, runtime BSS relation matrix VA 0x16EC28 stride 28).
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: How fast a roster can be built toward the RR-09 caps: EXE ~1.5 technical applicants per class per day, engine roughly 2-3x that.
- FIX SKETCH: Replace with the EXE rule (per class: thin by rand(0..n/3+1), add min(rand(0..3), cap-total), hybrids/androids gated by relation >= 0), or at least cap mean arrivals at 1.5/class/day.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — The EXE and engine descriptions are accurate, but the impact claim fails against the benchmark's own data. The finding says the engine's rate is "roughly 2-3x" the EXE's, using 2.5-4.5 arrivals per class per day at relation 0-20. Relation 0 gives 1.7x, and +10 to +20 is never reached. Realized relations in 100 benchmark saves put the engine at about 1.4x the EXE for days 0-10, at parity for days 10-20, and at about half after day 20.

The direction of the strategy consequence flips. The EXE's technical supply is independent of org hostility, so takeovers never starve recruitment there. In the engine they do. The fix sketch's fallback is also wrong: a 1.5 per day arrival cap that keeps the 33% churn would give a pool of about 4.6, harsher than the EXE's about 7. The finding's "equilibrium ~6" is also loose: my simulation gives about 7.0 after arrivals and about 5.5 before them.
  - CORRECTED: The verdict "diverges" holds, but the magnitude and direction in the strategy-impact claim do not. EXE (UFO2P.EXE, ISO non-4, FUN_0007a474 at file 0xDCB18): generation is flat and relation-independent for all four classes. Per class per day, thin applicants by rand[0..floor(n/3)+1], then add min(rand[0..3], cap-(employed+remaining)) new applicants, about 1.5 per day when the cap is not binding. Only Hybrids (Mutant Alliance, relation >= 0) and Androids (S.E.L.F., relation >= 0) are relation-gated, at rand[0..1] each. The engine instead gives each class 10 relation-weighted rolls per day with 33% churn, and none from hostile or taken-over orgs.

The engine is not "roughly 2-3x" the EXE in benchmark play. At relation 0 it is 2.57 per class per day, about 1.7x. Reaching 2.5-4.5 needs relation +10 to +20, which never occurs in benchmark play. Expected engine arrivals computed from the relations in 100 benchmark saves (INFERRED from the engine formula, not observed arrivals) average about 2.1 per technical class on days 0-10, about 1.3-1.5 on days 10-20 (parity with the EXE), and about 0.65-0.85 after day 20. After day 20, 84-95% of saves are below the EXE's 1.5. The cause is takeovers: in a day-34 save, 14 of the 21 hiring orgs are taken over and hostile, so Mechanics come from Civilian alone (about 0.26 per day).

The standing pools are nearly equal at relation 0: about 7.0 for the EXE (about 5.5 before the day's arrivals) and about 7.8 for the engine. The EXE thins more gently,

Both programs persist a 0xD4-byte mission record as `scenario/scenarir.dat`, and TACP's mission-end evaluator writes +0x98 as +1 (win), 0 or -1 (loss). Separately, a scan of UFO2P found exactly one code writer for each of the three alien-system flags (`0xDE59E`, `0xDE5A8`, `0xDE5B2`), all in `FUN_000b32ac`, so the unlock cannot happen elsewhere for a manned UFO. The only corrections are an inconsistency in the finding's own write-up (`blocked_on_re` and impact), the incomplete fix (vehicle loot), and the wrong repo doc named in the claim. - The 164 benchmark saves all carry alienIncidents as non-positive multiples of 30 (153 nonzero). That count comes from the validator's saves_scan.json, so it corroborates rather than independently verifies. My direct read of build-funding was denied.

The three corrections above fix statements around the value, not the value itself. The one that matters downstream is the fix_sketch, because the engine-fix plan would act on it literally.

### v_score / SCORE-C0 — Category 0 Tactical Missions: formula of the per-battle score added to the campaign score
- impact **medium**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence medium), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=True
- ENGINE: LIVE battle.cpp:3211-3212 (HEAD same) adds BattleScore::getTotal() to total and week. BattleScore (battle.cpp:4071-4080): total = combatRating + casualtyPenalty + leadership + liveAlienCaptured + equipmentCaptured + equipmentLost; leadership = (100 + 3*casualtyPenalty)*(combatRating + friendlyFire + liveAlienCaptured)/100 with no clamp; combatRating += type->score per hostile kill (battleunit.cpp:5112-5114); casualtyPenalty -= type->score per player death (5107); friendlyFire -= 10 flat per teamkill (5055); killing non-hostile non-own units has no score effect; liveAlienCaptured adds the species item score when containment exists; equipmentLost charged on destruction (battleitem.cpp:34).
- EXE: UFO2P adds [0x24408C] (TACP's debrief Total) at the start of every battle result. TACP: per death of a unit of value v (agent_type score, byte unit+0x44): killer hostile to victim => M[killerSide][victimSide]+=v, otherwise M[killerSide][victimSide]-=v (civilians, other non-hostile sides, own side); always C[victimSide]+=v. Combat rating = sum of X-COM's matrix row; leadership base = sum of its POSITIVE entries plus v for each incapacitated hostile when the mission is won (aliens only enter the base, others also enter the combat rating); leadership bonus = 0 when 30*C > 1000 (C >= 34) else base*(100-3C)/100; Total = combat rating - C + leadership + equipment captured - equipment lost (live-alien and corpse lines are 0 in the function). Brainsucker conversion (FUN_0006cf64) gives the alien side 2v and takes 2v from the X-COM row plus v casualty, matching Wong's 'double points' rule.
- EVIDENCE: TACP.EXE non-4: FUN_00022f90 (entry file 0x7DA34, VA 0x22F90, code file = VA + 0x5AAA4), FUN_00060720 (file 0xBB1C4), FUN_0006cf64 (file 0xC7A08), exporter FUN_00015494 (file 0x6FF38, '_DAT_001ed334 = DAT_0027a0a4'); UFO2P.EXE non-4 call at file 0x115967 in FUN_000b32ac (loads [0x17408C] at 0x115961). Unit value set at TACP file 0x7A36A ('mov [ebx+0x44], al' from agent_type_data +0 and +1). Link between the TACP export field and UFO2P [0x24408C] is inferred, not traced.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Tactical scoring: kills credit v each (Anthropod 10, Skeletoid 14, Psimorph/Megaspawn 30, Queenspawn 50), each dead agent costs 10 plus a leadership multiplier of (100-3C)%; at 4 or more dead agents the EXE leadership bonus is 0 while the engine makes it negative; civilian kills cost their value in the EXE and nothing in the engine. Lifetime tactical score also sets alien infantry gear (see SCORE-EQLEVEL).
- FIX SKETCH: In BattleScore::getLeadershipBonus: return 0 when casualty points >= 34 (i.e. 30*C > 1000) and build the base from positive kill credit only plus stunned/captured hostile values; subtract a victim's score from combatRating when a player unit kills a non-hostile non-own unit (civilians); drop friendlyFire from the leadership base and apply it to combatRating instead; re-check equipment captured/lost against the end-state rule once the TACP item table is decoded.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — The finding's formula was read from a decompile that silently dropped reachable code. Ghidra printed 'Removing unreachable block' for VA 0x23380-0x233B7 (the stunned-alien block) and for 0x23257-0x232E1 (the alive-hostile block). It folded the hand-off globals [0x1ED2C8], [0x1ED2CC], [0x1ED2F4] and [0x1ED350] to constants, because TACP code only reads them. The raw listing, which the validator also saved in gF.out, shows the blocks exist and are live. That makes 'live-alien and corpse lines are 0', 'Total = CR - C + leadership + equipment' and 'aliens only enter the leadership base' false.

The consequence: difference #3 in the finding ('EXE aliens: leadership base only') is wrong. The EXE and the engine share the same two-place structure (Total and base) and the same amounts. The true live-alien differences are the containment gate and the missing non-alien stun credit.

Everything else survives my independent check:
- The engine description is exact (function, condition and constant), and reachable from the running game.
- The clamp: C*30 > 1000 gives 0, as stated.
- The killer/victim matrix logic: confirmed, and the argument order the validator left unresolved is now resolved.
-
  - CORRECTED: Verdict stays 'diverges', but the EXE half of SCORE-C0 is mis-stated and one of its five listed differences (#3, live aliens) is false. The engine description is exactly right. LIVE and HEAD are identical for every score line. BattleScore sits at HEAD 4064-4073 and LIVE 4071-4080 because of LIVE's +7 lines at battle.cpp 3581.

CORRECT EXE RULE (TACP.EXE non-4 FUN_00022f90, file 0x7DA34, VA 0x22F90; called with the player org):
Total (VA 0x27A0A4) = Sum_k M[X][k] (the whole row, so civilian, non-hostile and teamkill entries subtract)
 + Sum v of incapacitated NON-alien hostiles
 - C
 + leadership
 + Sum v of incapacitated Alien-org units
 + equipment captured - equipment lost.
Leadership base = Sum of the POSITIVE row entries + Sum v of incapacitated hostiles of any org (on a won mission). Leadership = 0 if C >= 34, else base*(100-3C)/100.

Stunned aliens therefore pay v directly (Part F) AND v again through the leadership base (Part C). The engine does the same in two places: liveAlienCaptured is in both the Total and the base. The engine's *_ALIVE item scores equal the agent_type scores for all 13 species, so the amounts match.

The EXE credits stunned aliens regardless of containment. Part F is gated only by [0x1ED2C8]==1, which UFO2P always writes (see evidence). The flag [0x1ED350] only picks the display line: Live Aliens [0x27A098] if non-zero, else Combat Rating [0x27A088].

The engine pays only with bio-containment (battle.cpp:2897-2902) and pays nothing for stunned no

### v_score / SCORE-UNLOCK-CRAFT — Alien craft module gating by lifetime UFO-kill score (rows 4-7 of the 9x5 unlock table)
- impact **medium**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence high), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: LIVE vehicle.cpp:3928-3951 (HEAD 3895): equipDefaultEquipment strips a module from an aliens-owned craft's default loadout when totalScore.craftShotDownUFO < etype->scoreRequirementFor(state.difficulty); the four thresholds are the extracted cequip_score_req_data rows (Superhuman 1000, 2000, 4000, 8000); loot lists only modules actually equipped. Re-evaluated continuously, and at every gate re-entry (vehiclemission.cpp:1437). The 5th row is deliberately unbound.
- EXE: At the Monday rollover FUN_00097b90 sets nine saved flags [raw 0x10B84+k] from a 9x5 u32 table (difficulty = word 0x10C9C): row k compared with lifetime cat 3 (week+previous) for k=4..8. Superhuman thresholds: 1000 Small Disruption Shield (vequip 44), 2000 Large Disruption Shield (45), 4000 Cloaking Field (46), 8000 Teleporter (47), 16000 Dimension Shifter (48). Flags are only set, never cleared. FUN_000b3e84 consumes them when creating Alien-owned craft (locked module slots in vehicle_data.loaded_equipment_slots are cleared) and when building UFO recovery loot. Row 8 is inert: none of the ten UFO records loads module 48; the module lists per UFO type equal the engine's.
- EVIDENCE: UFO2P.EXE non-4: latch at file 0xFA27E-0xFA2E5 inside FUN_00097b90 (entry file 0xFA234); table file 0x142174 (9 rows x 5 u32, read directly: rows 4-8 = 2000/1750/1500/1250/1000, 4000/..., 8000/..., 16000/..., 32000/28000/24000/20000/16000); flags initial bytes file 0x142228; consumer FUN_000b3e84 (entry file 0x116528; jump table items 44..48 -> flags 4..8) called at file 0xC04A0 (FUN_0005d6e4 vehicle creation, entry file 0xBFD88) and file 0x115C82 (FUN_000b32ac, mission type 3 loot); item names from vehicle_equipment_names at file 1353641. Retraction: an earlier exact-slice scan of 0x1421C4 found no reference because the table base is 0x142174.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Shooting down UFOs arms later UFOs: at Superhuman shields appear at 1000/2000 lifetime UFO-kill points, cloaking at 4000, teleporters at 8000. The EXE only sees a new total after the next Monday rollover and also filters recovery loot by the flags; the engine acts immediately. Benchmark runs stay far below 1,000.
- FIX SKETCH: Add 9 saved latch bytes to GameState; set them in the weekly rollover from lifetime cat 3 (and cat 0 for the first four) using the 9x5 table (rename the 5th row Dimension Shifter, harmless); have equipDefaultEquipment and UFO loot construction read the latches instead of the live score.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — The finding's central EXE claim is wrong. It says FUN_00097b90 sets the flags "at the Monday rollover" and "the EXE only sees a new total after the next Monday rollover". Both the disassembly and Ghidra show FUN_00097b90 is called every day. In FUN_0004ac40 (file 0xAD2E4, VA 0x4AC40) the weekday-0 branch (file 0xAD478, VA 0x4ADD4) guards only funding and its companions. The paths rejoin at file 0xAD4A5 (VA 0x4AE01), which calls FUN_00097b90 (file 0xAD4B8, VA 0x4AE14) on every day change. Inside FUN_00097b90, `cmp word [0x4d64],0 / jne` (file 0xFA23A) gates only the fold loop. The latch (file 0xFA27E-0xFA2E5) always runs, and week+previous is invariant under the fold. A second caller, FUN_0004ac0c (file 0xAD2B0, tail-jump at file 0xAD2C3), is reached from file 0x7427C, after that routine's own weekday-0 funding gate, so it is unconditional too. The finding's "up to a week" lag, its "medium" impact and its fix sketch ("set them in the weekly rollover") all rest on that error. The rest verified: the 9x5 table, thresholds, comparison direction, consumer and module sets are right (details in evidence). The engine description is mostly right, but "re-evaluated continuously" is inaccurate
  - CORRECTED: The thresholds, comparison, module sets and alien-only scope all check out, but the EXE latches daily, not on Monday. FUN_00097b90 (UFO2P non-4, entry file 0xFA234) is called at every game-day change. Only the week-to-previous score fold inside it is gated to weekday 0. The nine flags are re-tested against lifetime week+previous every midnight, so a crossing is visible to the EXE after at most 24 game-hours, not up to a week. Corrected rule: at Superhuman, shields at 1000 (Small) and 2000 (Large), Cloaking at 4000 and Teleporter at 8000 lifetime UFO-kill points arm alien craft created or recovered after the next midnight. The engine reads the live total with no latch, so the divergence is at most one game-day of timing. Strategy impact is low, not medium. Two engine-side items are unproven rather than established divergences. First, the engine also re-equips at incursion launch and at every non-player portal crossing; the EXE's only two consumers are creation and recovery loot, and it was not established whether the EXE re-creates craft per incursion or crossing. Second, EXE recovery loot is built from the UFO type's static module list filtered by the flags at recovery time, while the engine's loot is whatever passed the gate when the craft was last equipped. The engine is evaluated at weekly growth, incursion launch and portal crossing, not "continuously". Fix sketch: if a latch is wanted at all, evaluate it in the end-of-day tick (updateEndOfDay), not the weekly rollover.

- FIX SKETCH: none.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — The level mapping is correct, but the finding's exe_behaviour includes "a random row of 10 per weapon/grenade/equipment group" and then says match / fix none. The engine does not do that. The finding never examines how the engine samples; the write-up has no mention of empty rows. I found the divergence in the EXE code, the extractor code and the benchmark save. Separately, I confirmed the validator's penalty reading (N = FUN_0006e608(1), n5 = FUN_0006ea78(1,5), level reduced when n5 < N/10) against the asm. That is the SCORE-EQPENALTY finding, so it does not change this verdict.
  - CORRECTED: The level-selection half of SCORE-EQLEVEL holds: the 5x7 threshold table, the lifetime tactical-score input, the species gating, and the count-of-thresholds-<=-score rule (score exactly on a threshold goes up a level) all match. The verdict is "diverges", not "match", and "fix: none" is wrong, because the gear drawn within a level differs. The EXE draws one of 10 rows independently for each of weapon, grenade and equipment, and an empty row issues nothing. The engine extractor drops empty grenade/equipment rows, so the engine draws only among non-empty rows and always issues an item whenever any row is non-empty. P(grenade issued), EXE vs engine, at Superhuman: score 1000-2200 (ALIEN_2) 0.3 vs 1.0; 2200-4000 (ALIEN_3) 0.6 vs 1.0; 4000-6600 (ALIEN_4) 0.8 vs 1.0. P(equipment issued): 6600-10000 (ALIEN_5, Personal Disruptor Shield) 0.7 vs 1.0. All other level/group cells match, and weapons match at every level (all 10 rows are filled). The probabilities are conditional on the unit having a free slot; the engine's slot-fit rule was not compared. Fix: keep the 10-row denominator. Either keep empty rows as null entries in EquipmentSet, or draw r in [0,10) and issue list[r] only if r < list.size(). Fix is in `tools/extractors/extract_agent_equipment.cpp` lines 135/143 (alien) and 1093/1101 (human; same compaction, a lead only) plus `EquipmentSet::generateEquipmentList`, `game/state/rules/aequipmenttype.cpp` ~62-88. blocked_on_re: false. Strategy impact is low for the current benchma

The generated Psimorph data has psi_energy 170, and nothing caps it.

EXE side: I re-read the FUN_0001f830 and FUN_000ad93c listings from my own Ghidra queries. Accuracy is (b+r)*(100-4D)/100 with IDIV truncation, with b at rec+0x1A and inc at rec+0x1B. My arithmetic reproduces every number in the finding (Anthropod 0->16, Skeletoid 30->42, Spitter 38->48, Megaspawn 50->58, turret 60->67, guards 20-40 -> 33-50, Anthropod psi defence 49, Psimorph psi energy 100).

UFO2P non-4 FUN_00015400 (VA 0x15400, file 0x77AA4) is the new-game alien seeding routine. It reads the difficulty word d to size the initial alien infestation:
- It picks a random building whose owner has relation to X-COM >= 0.
- It sets building crew[1] = 1 + rand16(d/2+1), a range of 1..d/2+2.
- It sets crew[5] = 1 + rand16(d/2+2), a range of 1..d/2+3.
- rand16(n) is inclusive [0,n].
- Species mapping is INFERRED (see evidence). crew[1] is the Brainsucker (alien speed 1) and crew[5] is the Anthropod (alien speed 2).

At Superhuman (d=4) the EXE seeds a Brainsucker count of 1..4 and an Anthropod count of 1..5. The engine seeds 1..3 for each. At every difficulty the engine is one lower for the Brainsucker, and one or two lower for the Anthropod:
- Engine: extractor `Vec2{1, d/2+2}` (extract_agent_types.cpp:1332-1337), consumed via `randBoundsExclusive` at gamestate.cpp:706 LIVE / 705 HEAD. That call returns [x, y-1].
- Mean seed infiltration numerator (sum of crew × alienSpeed, before building and org multipliers) at d=4: about 8.5 in the EXE vs 6.0 in the engine, a gap of about 42%. The maximum is 14 vs 9.

Corrected strategy rule: Superhuman does two things.
- It speeds hourly infiltration growth by 42/38 versus Beginner.
- It seeds a larger starting

It is gated on the agent type's inventory flag.

The validator's description of the structure holds, and its data check reproduces: 45 records, 0 mismatches against ufo_incursions.xml, with craft index mapping PROBE=0 ... MOTHERSHIP=9. But the claim that the engine is "equivalent, given that the engine's three lists are the EXE's three slots split by role" is false for record 3. The engine builds `limits = vehicleLimits` and subtracts primary + attack + escort cumulatively (LIVE gamestate.cpp:958-975, HEAD 957-974; the invasion() region is otherwise identical in HEAD and LIVE, differing only by a 1-line offset). The EXE compares each slot to the pool with no running total, and the extractor puts record 3's two Battleship slots into primaryList and attackList. invasion() is reachable: GameState::update() calls it at LIVE gamestate.cpp:1399 when gameTime > nextInvasion and no battle is active. The only other nextInvasion references outside gamestate.cpp are the header, the serializer and an introspection print. The higher-level claims (priority order, ranges, no retry, timer reset regardless via FUN_0006d384 and FUN_0004ab04, both of which call FUN_0006cfb4 with EAX = 0) are cor
  - CORRECTED: F2 is a match for the structure and the data, but not for the fit test on every package, so "match" and "equivalent" overclaim. What holds: (a) priority = index order inside the mission-type range ([0,20) Infiltration, [20,30) Attack, [30,40) Subversion, [40,45) Overspawn); (b) no retry with another type or week when nothing fits; (c) the timer resets either way; (d) all 45 records match the EXE, with 0 mismatches in mission type, priority and craft/count/role. What is wrong: the EXE tests each of the 3 slots independently against the pool (pool[craft] >= count[slot], signed, no running total) and then subtracts sequentially with no clamp. The engine sums counts per vehicle type across primaryList + attackList + escortList and requires the sum to fit. The two rules differ only for a record that repeats a craft type across slots. Exactly one of the 45 does: record 3 = UFO_INCURSION_I4 (Battleship x1 role 7, Escort x1 role 11, Battleship x1 role 5). EXE condition: Battleship >= 1 and Escort >= 1. Engine condition: Battleship >= 2 and Escort >= 1. The divergence appears only when I1-I3 have already failed, which means Mothership = 0, Escort = exactly 1 and Battleship = exactly 1, on an Infiltration roll. In that case the EXE launches I4 and drives pool[Battleship] to -1 (signed word). A negative pool then blocks every Battleship package until growth refills it. The engine rejects I4 and falls through to I5 and later. The engine pool can never go negative. Strategy impact: low (o

### v_victory_chain / VC-12 — Auto-routing of craft arriving in the alien dimension
- impact **medium**, validator verdict **engine_missing** (confidence medium), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=False
- ENGINE: Absent: arriving craft take no target (the harness must issue the order).
- EXE: After placing an arriving vehicle in the alien city, the EXE loops the city's buildings in array order and orders the craft to the first building for which the raid test passes; if none, it patrols a random point near the gate.
- EVIDENCE: FUN_0006eea4 (file 0xD1548): loop VA 0x6F15A-0x6F17F calls FUN_0004a33c on each building (IMUL 0xE2, bound [0x183A68]) and sets vehicle +0xCF=0, +0xD0=idx; fallback +0xCF=2 with a random point (VA 0x6F196-0x6F1E4).
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: In the original the first arrival heads for the next researched building automatically; the harness must issue the landing order itself in the engine.
- FIX SKETCH: On arrival in CITYMAP_ALIEN, order the craft to the first alien building whose access topic is complete and which is alive.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — Short version: the EXE evidence checks out. The engine claim "arriving craft take no target" is wrong in a way that matters for where the fix goes. The strategy-rule sentence is wrong for the first arrival. I changed the verdict from engine_missing to diverges because the engine does implement the fallback branch.

I checked three things independently.

1. EXE side: confirmed. I re-read the FUN_0006eea4 listing, the jump table, and the helpers.
- The loop at VA 0x6F156-0x6F17F is as the finding describes. It exits at the first building where FUN_0004a33c returns nonzero.
- The fallback is +0xCF=2 with x and y from rand(10)+pos-5.
- The jump table in the EXE (CRC 0x4749ffc1) is ascending, f0 to f9.
- I decompiled the helpers myself. FUN_0004db84 takes a building index and the craft's x, y, z and scans the building's bounding box. It returns the tile that has the entrance flag (byte +0xE) and is nearest to the craft, plus a valid flag.
- FUN_0004e0d4 sets the craft's destination.
- The player's click-on-building handler (FUN_0004b2a4, VA 0x4B739-0x4B770) uses the identical sequence, so the auto-route is the same as ordering the craft to the building.
- FUN_00049a08 is the alien-map d
  - CORRECTED: The EXE half of VC-12 holds. The engine half ("Absent: arriving craft take no target") is wrong, and so is the strategy-rule sentence. Corrected version: In the EXE, FUN_0006eea4 (UFO2P non-4, file 0xD1548) runs for every vehicle placed after a crossing, not only X-COM craft, because FUN_0006d384 has no owner test in its loop. Placement is followed by a two-branch order. Branch 1: the first building in array order that passes FUN_0004a33c gets +0xCF=0, +0xD0=idx, and a destination of its nearest entrance tile via FUN_0004db84 and FUN_0004e0d4; this is the same call sequence as the player's click-on-building order. Branch 2, if no building passes: a one-shot goto (+0xCF=2) to a random point from -5 to +4 tiles of the arrival position. The engine does give an arriving X-COM craft an order. It sends it to a random gotoLocation within +/-2 tiles of the portal (ArriveFromDimensionGate case 0, LIVE:game/state/city/vehiclemission.cpp:2707 calls takePositionNearPortal, body at :3580-3585; zoneMode defaults to -1 at vehiclemission.h:299-300, so the incursion branch is not taken). That is the equivalent of the EXE's branch 2. Only branch 1 (building targeting) is missing, so the verdict is partial, 'diverges', not wholly 'engine_missing'. The fix point is the takePositionNearPortal call in that case. For the strategy rule: on the very first crossing no building passes FUN_0004a33c, so the original also sends the first arrival to a random point near the gate. Record 92 needs record 1 co

### v_victory_chain / VC-13 — Timing of entering and returning from the dimension (midnight switch, 24 h return)
- impact **medium**, validator verdict **exe_unbound** (confidence low), review **not_refuted_by_policy**, blocked_on_re=True
- ENGINE: View switches at the day change while any player craft is in the other city (LIVE:game/ui/tileview/cityview.cpp:2846-2891; HEAD :2771-2816). Return delay uniform 1-2 h, entry 1-2 s (LIVE:vehiclemission.cpp:1417-1432; HEAD :1414-1429).
- EXE: Not resolved. OBSERVED: crossing sets vehicle city byte +0x174 to 0xFF and +0x172 to the destination; FUN_000105b8 converts 0xFF to 0xFE; FUN_0006d384 places vehicles with byte 0xFE whose destination equals the current city via FUN_0006eea4 with no countdown seen in any of these functions. UFOpaedia 'arrive at midnight' and 'at least 24 hours' are not visible there.
- EVIDENCE: FUN_0003a910 (file 0x9CFB4) sets +0x174/+0x172 at file 0x9D147-0x9D15F; FUN_000105b8 file 0x72C5C; FUN_0006d384 file 0xCFA28 (arrival branch VA 0x6D544-0x6D7C3); FUN_00010908 (file 0x72FAC) decompiled but not read for time gating.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Sortie cadence (how long a team is away; whether to send craft before midnight). Treat the 24 h return as folklore until bound.
- FIX SKETCH: none until bound; most promising next lead is FUN_00010908 and FUN_000114cc.

### v_victory_chain / VC-16 — Alien dimension fleet persistence between visits
- impact **medium**, validator verdict **diverges** (confidence medium), review **refuted**, blocked_on_re=True
- ENGINE: Alien-city vehicles persist as real Vehicle objects; damage persists (GH-1317 per web_alien_campaign 6.3, unverified in code). updateUfoGrowth spawns real vehicles into CITYMAP_ALIEN (LIVE:gamestate.cpp:1617-1700).
- EXE: The dimension is kept as per-type counts. On each switch into it FUN_0006e098 tops up live UFOs to the stored per-type counts (freshly initialised vehicles). While no X-COM craft is present FUN_000105b8 removes every alien-owned vehicle of that city. So every visit meets a full-health fleet. Not observed whether kills decrement the stored count.
- EVIDENCE: FUN_0006e098 (file 0xD073C) spawn loop using word [0x143A6C+city*0x2D4+type*2] and FUN_0006de64; FUN_000105b8 (file 0x72C5C) FUN_00058280 loop over vehicles with +0x12C==1 in a city holding no X-COM craft.
- STRATEGY RULE AFFECTED: Whether it is worth shooting UFOs in the dimension, and how to estimate the stock available to the VC-10 mass assault.
- FIX SKETCH: Model the dimension as counts and respawn on entry; first bind whether a kill reduces the stock.
- REFUTER (engine): refuted=True verdict_should_be=diverges — The EXE structure the validator describes holds: per-type counts, top-up on switch, and a cleanup loop in FUN_000105b8. The conclusion drawn from it, and the claim that the kill question is blocked on RE, do not survive the validator's own cited functions. (1) FUN_00058280, the removal routine FUN_000105b8 calls, itself DECs the stock array being discussed (stock[currentCity][type], for owner 1 and type < 10). The validator reported "not observed whether kills decrement the stored count" without reading that function; it is observable and the answer is yes. (2) The validator conflates two unload paths. The silent one (no stock change) is FUN_00070694, called from FUN_0006e098 when the old city is non-zero. The one it cites, in FUN_000105b8, goes through the decrementing FUN_00058280. Taken at face value the cited path would drain the stock on exit, the opposite of "full fleet". The main-loop order suggests the silent path normally runs first, but that was not proven. (3) The engine description omits GameState::invasion consuming the dimension fleet. The EXE does the same in FUN_0006cfb4, which subtracts launched counts from stock[1]; the validator's disp:143a6c scan missed this bec
  - CORRECTED: VC-16 should read: the EXE keeps the alien dimension as a persistent per-type population count (stock[1][type], word array at VA 0x143D40, i.e. 0x143A6C + city*0x2D4). Kills, incursions and weekly growth all act on that count, so they persist. Only the survivors' health and position are reset. On leaving, the live objects are freed silently (FUN_00070694 inside the city switch FUN_0006e098). On re-entry FUN_0006e098 spawns (count - live) fresh craft with HP = vehicle_data constitution. Engine (LIVE) is the same in kind: it also has kill attrition, incursion consumption (GameState::invasion takes the UFOs out of CITYMAP_ALIEN) and weekly capped growth. The real divergence is narrow: the engine keeps damaged survivors damaged and never resets their position, because nothing restores health (grep of vehicle.cpp: health is written only at :1952, :2052, :3952) and only the current city is updated. The EXE re-spawns survivors at full HP. So "every visit meets a full-health fleet" is true only for health. The fleet SIZE is not reset; it is the persistent stock after kills and incursions, plus growth. Strategy: shooting UFOs in the dimension is worthwhile (kills reduce the stock; the harness should finish kills in one visit, since wounding is wasted). Stock available for a mass assault = capped weekly growth minus incursions minus kills, not a fresh full fleet. Impact: low-medium, not medium. blocked_on_re: false for "do kills decrement" (answered yes). Still open (exe_unbound sub-qu

- TACP.EXE (ISO non-4): bind the end-of-mission item recovery (loot capture, cargo-capacity requirement, where items go); start from the savegame.dat string hit at TACP VA 0xE30E5 and the scenario/ string users FUN_00011A90 and FUN_00014008.
- Decode the register arguments of the two FUN_0005faf0 calls inside FUN_000b32ac (VA 0xB3xxx, worth tiers) to settle whether the tiered delta hits owner->X-COM or X-COM->owner.
- Callers of FUN_000ac08c (file 0x10E730; the O2 note places seven in the vehicle-mission family rooted at FUN_0003a910): map the UI Raid and Exterminate actions to launcher types 3 and 4 to settle which one forces rel[X-COM][owner] <= -60.
- FUN_00091db0 has 34 call sites (validate/raw/b14_hostile_callers.txt): decode the argument order at the combat sites (FUN_00054a28 x4, FUN_00041644, FUN_0004e48c, FUN_00058b88, FUN_0007a730) to learn what a persistent rel[X-COM][org] <= -60 changes for X-COM craft.
- Count loot spots present in a generated Temple of Sirius battle (engine sector placement battlemap.cpp:548-880, or a real raid save): every $/raid figure scales with it; I did not re-derive the prior's 23 total or 14.8 expected.
- Decode FUN_00090d68 (file 0xF340C) fully: what the non-0/1 class purchase path credits and whether any seller balance moves.
- Verify the per-base lab cap enforcement function (string file 0x152252 has no direct xref) and the Advanced Workshop slot count (10) and engineer skill (60-100) that the 400 man-hours/hour workshop throughput assumes.
- Find the EXE consumer that sets a newly manufactured craft's health (assumed full) to confirm craft are sold at full value.

### v_tactical_difficulty (og-research:export/strategy-plan-2026-10-08/validate/v_tactical_difficulty.md)
- UFO2P writer of the tactical header block (TACP VA 0x1ED294, 212 bytes) that holds difficulty [0x1ED340], reinforcement interval [0x1ED344], mission-type fields [0x1ED2CC]/[0x1ED2F4], flags [0x1ED2C8]/[0x1ED350]/[0x1ED32C]: the TACP block descriptor table (VA 0x27F00C, 10-byte {size,addr,flag} entries; entry 4 = 1966-byte BUILDING.DAT, entry 5 = this 212-byte block) does not line up with the savegame.00 writer order parsed from UFO2P FUN_000a3234/FUN_000a4054; candidates FUN_00016c67, FUN_000b3ee0, FUN_000b0380, FUN_000ac304 (called at the end of FUN_000ac348).
- Per-building reinforcement intervals (5..40 s): not in either EXE; trace the header builder above or per-mission parameters.
- UFO2P tier handicap: table at VA 0x143A7E (FUN_0006e608 / FUN_0006ea78 called from FUN_000ad38c).
- Base-defence capture routing: FUN_000b44a4 virtual carrier [0x1304CC] and whether Alien Containment capacity is enforced.
- Org tech-level consumer in tactical gear and mapping of equipment-set ids 13..18 and 8..12 onto the fork's EquipmentSets; which of the bucket path (FUN_00024e5c) or tier path (FUN_000250f8) Anthropod/Skeletoid use.
- Hit-chance function using the unit accuracy byte +0x8E (FUN_00055400 stat routine computes 100-byte; firing code not decoded): needed to prove the effective-accuracy direction numerically.
- Turret ammo/slot semantics of FUN_00059f54 (slot arg 2 for plasma pods) and the real plasma turret shot count.
- Post-battle money/relation handler FUN_000b32ac (file 0x115950): X-COM balance -50*DAT_00244090 on mission type 2 win and an org relation delta -(x-50)/20 (x<=250) / -10-(x-250)/50 (cap -50) for types 1/4; DAT_00244090 undecoded (possibly building damage).
- Steam '4' builds (TACP4.EXE, UFO2P4.EXE) not examined; no equivalence claimed.
- Erratum for the docs: Mind Shield +30 / cap 200 is inline in TACP FUN_00066474 (file 0xC0F18), not at the non-resolving FUN_0009b780.

### v_score (og-research:export/strategy-plan-2026-10-08/validate/v_score.md)
- Alien-city structure table (UFO2P non-4, per-city struct VA 0x1439E0 + city*0x2D4, 40 entries x 14 bytes at +0x9E, type word at entry+2): find the writers that fill entries initially (new game / FUN_0006e098 city-1 load) and the ones that remove entries on a won raid (FUN_000b3d4c collapse, FUN_000b4538 unlock writer), and decode the other entry fields; needed to confirm type 5 = Organic Factory and to port the FUN_000ad38c level penalty.
- TACP hand-off: confirm TACP [0x10D34C] (alien equipment score) is filled from UFO2P _DAT_002440A4 and that UFO2P [0x24408C] equals TACP _DAT_001ed334 (trace the four FUN_00013e64 chunk writes in TACP FUN_00015494, file 0x6FF38); also settle which of UFO2P FUN_000ad38c and TACP FUN_000250f8 equips a given raid garrison.
- TACP equipment captured/lost terms: decode the 750-record item table at TACP VA 0xEA2BC (0x30-byte records, bytes +0x02 org, +0x03 carrier, +0x01 value) to settle the exact end-state rule used in FUN_00022f90 and compare with battleitem.cpp scoreAsLost.
- TACP relation threshold byte at VA 0x2818B8 (hostile) and the argument order of FUN_00021998 inside FUN_00060720: confirm -50 and whether the hostile test is killer-to-victim or victim-to-killer for civilians and Megapol/Cult kills.
- What FUN_00080528 / FUN_0007fdb0 / FUN_00080418 represent (tile crush, explosion, building collapse) to size how often an X-COM craft is lost with no attacker (category 4 difference).
- First-detection multiplier in FUN_0006f964: identify [0xE0E7C] (saved dword), word [0xD4D64] and word [0xD4D68] (date words?) behind 'x100 detection if [0xE0E7C]==1 && [0xD4D64]==1 && [0xD4D68] < 16' and compare with the engine's unbounded firstDetection.
- Whether TACP applies unlock flags 0-3 to built-in sets 8-12 for inventory aliens (rows 0-3 of the 9x5 table): examine TACP's built-in set loader and its callers.
- Errata to carry into exe_evidence_index.md: FUN_000ad148 (file 0x10F7EC) is the built-in equipment-set loader (92-byte set records at VA 0xF5248, roster counter [0x2440C4] capped at 500), not the invasion-timer init (that is FUN_0004ab04 [0xD5068]); cequip_score_req_data at file 0x1421C4 is rows 4-8 of the 9x5 table at file 0x142174 and its 5th row is Dimension Shifter.
